### Phase 8A — Private room domain and persistence

- **Objective:** implement the durable private-room lifecycle and membership invariants.
- **Scope:** create/read/join/leave/start application ports, `WAITING -> FULL -> STARTING -> STARTED -> CLOSED`, waiting/full expiry, capacity, host transfer and idempotent commands. Per owner approval, REST exposes create/read only; Socket wiring remains Phase 12A.
- **Expected deliverables:** Rooms domain/application/repository adapter and authenticated `/v1/rooms` create/read contracts.
- **Dependencies:** Phases 7A/7B; live activation remains gated by 7C and gameplay readiness.
- **Acceptance criteria:** only the host can request start of a full eligible room; concurrent joins cannot exceed capacity; leave/host-transfer transitions are deterministic; repeated commands cannot repeat effects.
- **Required tests and validations:** state-machine unit tests, PostgreSQL integration tests, concurrent join/idempotency/expiry tests, REST authorization/contract tests, lint/typecheck/build.
- **Status:** Completed
- **Assessment / resolved pause (2026-09-22):** began on clean `main` at `9936da1` (Phase 7B), read the four required sources using the documented canonical architecture path, and verified only relevant Users/Games ports and Rooms storage/ownership. No earlier completed prerequisite required reimplementation. Initially paused because architecture section 15.1 listed REST membership routes while section 10.2 and AGENTS.md assigned them to Socket. The owner approved REST create/read plus application-port join/leave/start and subsequently requested continuation. [ADR-019](adr/019-private-room-foundation.md) records the resolution; architecture section 15.1 is reconciled. The earlier plan-only blocked assessment is superseded by this completion.
- **Delivered (2026-09-27):** pure Rooms transitions, injected clock, Users/Games admission checks, member-only safe snapshots, lowest-free-seat allocation, deterministic oldest-member host transfer, empty-room closure, lazy/idempotent expiry and trusted Match confirmation/close callbacks. Serializable PostgreSQL transactions with bounded retries atomically commit room/member changes and minimal command receipts. Actor-scoped command IDs and canonical request digests preserve acknowledgements across retries/restarts and reject changed intents. Delayed join retries cannot rejoin a departed member; expected revisions protect start-versus-roster races.
- **Persistence / transport:** additive room schema/revision/started-match identity and member join revision, Rooms-owned receipt table and API insert/read-only receipt grants; legacy data survives with compatible defaults. The required invite column receives CSPRNG material but no invite is exposed or resolved. Added ROOM_CONFLICT/GAME_UNAVAILABLE, opt-in complete room runtime configuration without default lifetime/budgets, independent distributed create/read limits that fail closed, authenticated REST create/read, generated/frozen Rooms OpenAPI and compatibility checking. [Room operations](architecture/private-rooms.md) records rollout, rollback, trusted-port boundaries and limitations. No dependency/lockfile change.
- **Verification:** frozen-lockfile install; scoped API build/typecheck/test; Prisma format/validate/generation; OpenAPI initialization/generation/check; lint; final `pnpm check`; isolated `pnpm test:auth-http:local`; and diff checks passed. Final root gate: 1160 tests (580 API, 211 config, 48 Bot, 14 Worker, 158 architecture/tooling, 15 TypeScript, 17 database unit, 110 validation, 7 infra), 12 workspace typechecks and 11 builds, with unchanged tasks eligible for cache. Architecture audit covers 13 workspaces/114 source files; all five OpenAPI checks pass. The phase adds 53 root-gate cases and 13 integration cases, plus extensions to runtime/upgrade/restart checks.
- **Integration evidence:** all 110 PostgreSQL/Redis tests pass (16 database, 88 API, 6 Worker). Rooms covers concurrent fresh create, eight duplicate joins, last-seat contention, delayed retries after leave, host transfer, start-versus-leave/join/expiry, trusted start/close idempotency, inactive users, disabled production catalog, rollback after receipt insertion failure, receipt privileges, database closure and distributed quotas. Actual configured HTTP startup rejects room creation through the disabled catalog. Migration deploy/redeploy, zero Prisma-visible drift, populated legacy room/member expansion and room/member/receipt persistence across a real PostgreSQL restart pass. PostgreSQL 18.6 packages were extracted into a task-specific temporary directory, not installed system-wide; the harness cleans its disposable clusters/Redis containers.
- **Corrections during verification:** corrected test fixture literal/delegate typing; generated request JSON Schema using input mode to represent UUID normalization; initialized the new contract baseline after that correction; updated the pinned shared error-code assertion for the two additive codes. The deterministic sequence test now exercises repeated transitions rather than remaining in a terminal state. Final checks have no failures.
- **Limits / mandatory stop:** no invitation resolution/link sharing, Socket transport/events, UI, engine, Match creation, live activation, production migration, commit, push, deployment or live Bot change. ### Phase 9 — Matchmaking and cancel-vs-match safety

- **Objective:** implement Redis-coordinated matchmaking whose committed matches remain durable in PostgreSQL.
- **Scope:** ticket lifecycle, one active ticket per user/game/mode, atomic enqueue/cancel/claim, TTL reservation, DB commit/release compensation, admission token, and post-commit notification intent.
- **Expected deliverables:** Matchmaking module, Redis scripts/adapters, match-creation port, contracts, and race tests.
- **Dependencies:** Phase 8B and database/Redis foundations.
- **Acceptance criteria:** exactly one of `MATCHED` or `CANCELLED` wins; match-found is exposed only after DB commit; Redis loss cannot erase a committed match.
- **Required tests and validations:** unit/integration tests, cancel-vs-match concurrency tests, duplicate enqueue tests, Redis/DB failure tests, lint/typecheck/build.
- **Status:** Completed
- **Authorization / assessment (2026-09-27):** owner explicitly authorized Phase 9. Starting worktree was clean on `main` at `49f4083` (Phase 8B). Read the four required sources in full, using the documented canonical `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` resolution. Inspected relevant manifests/task graph, Users eligibility, Games catalog, Rooms ports, database schema/ownership, Redis adapter conventions and the isolated integration harness. No earlier completed prerequisite was found defective or requiring reimplementation. Matchmaking and Matches are expected empty public surfaces; the match-creation port is an explicit Phase 9 deliverable, not a missing earlier-phase prerequisite.
- **Historical decision gate (resolved):** architecture section 9.3 requires Redis TTL reservations, durable Match creation, compensation and exactly one cancellation/match winner, but does not specify cancellation during reservation or arbitration after an uncertain DB commit/Redis loss. A Redis-only release after timeout cannot establish that a delayed database transaction will not commit. The execution protocol's Handling Ambiguity section requires an owner decision before changes materially affecting persistence, security or public behavior. Pause before implementing the following proposed durable arbitration and cancellation contract; this is a Phase 9 design decision, not a claim that Phase 8B failed.
- **Approved policy:** Redis owns FIFO queue ordering, atomic enqueue/claim and TTL reservations partitioned by game/mode/capacity. A minimal Matches-owned durable admission record fences each stable ticket identity and reservation generation through its public application port. It records actor/game/mode, immutable intent identity, deadlines, generation and terminal disposition, without queue payloads, credentials or game state. Registration serializes active attempts for the same user/game/mode; changing capacity while an attempt is active conflicts. Matchmaking never queries Matches tables directly.
- **Approved cancellation / recovery behavior:** cancellation remains possible while reserved until the durable match-creation decision wins. Cancellation and creation serialize on the same admission records. A cancellation acknowledgement is returned only after durable cancellation; a creation transaction atomically records all winning assignments, pinned-version CREATED Match, players and token-free notification outbox intent. If cancellation wins for any selected ticket, that group cannot commit a Match; unaffected tickets can be released/requeued. If creation wins, Cancel returns the committed match disposition instead of CANCELLED. An uncertain commit is reconciled by stable identity against PostgreSQL before releasing/reusing an assignment; unavailable arbitration returns retryable unavailability, never a guessed terminal result. Expiry/new generations fence old claimants. Redis restart cannot erase a committed result or authorize a second match for the same ticket. Minimal terminal records remain available for idempotency; no automatic deletion/archival policy is introduced by this proposal.
- **Implementation boundary:** record the accepted decision in an ADR and implement/verify only Phase 9. Admission requires a current authenticated identity, committed membership and a short-lived token; credentials must not be stored in outbox or telemetry. Durations remain explicit configuration, not guessed production defaults. Preserve ADR-018: production catalog remains non-playable with null descriptor; isolated tests may supply executable-descriptor fixtures, but no production engine versions or modes are invented. CREATED match persistence is within Phase 9; gameplay transitions, outbox dispatch, Socket wiring and activation remain Phases 10/11/12 and 7C. The future descriptor integration must be completed before live admission.
- **Historical assessment-only result / verification:** only this plan is modified. No product implementation, dependency, schema/grant, accepted ADR, service startup, migration, commit, deployment or live Telegram change. `pnpm format:check` and `git diff --check` pass; final diff/status review confirms this is the sole modified file. Behavior/unit/integration/build/E2E checks were not run for this documentation-only assessment. Existing historical test results are not new evidence. Resume Phase 9 after the focused arbitration/cancellation decision, not Phase 10A.

- **Approval / implementation split (2026-09-27):** owner explicitly answered “Approved” to the durable arbitration/cancellation proposal. [ADR-021](adr/021-matchmaking-arbitration.md) records acceptance. Split Phase 9 into independently reviewable 9A (durable arbitration/atomic creation) and 9B (Redis orchestration/admission credentials). Complete only 9A in this interaction and stop at its gate. The parent phase is not complete until both subphases pass.

- **Completion (2026-09-28):** 9A and 9B are implemented and their available local verification passes. Matchmaking is usable through authenticated application ports and trusted ticks; production composition/activation, Socket transport and outbox dispatch remain explicitly deferred to the owning phases. Stop at the 9B report before 10A.

### Phase 9A — Durable admission arbitration and atomic match creation

- **Objective:** make cancellation, reservation generations and match creation mutually consistent in PostgreSQL before Redis integration.
- **Scope:** Matches-owned admission/reservation records; stable command identity, unique active user/game/mode slot, strict v1 application contracts, explicit deadlines, generation fencing, cancellation/release/reconciliation, CREATED Match/players/pinned versions and token-free outbox intent in one transaction.
- **Expected deliverables:** Matches public admission port, repository, additive migration/grants/ownership checks, ADR-021, boundary documentation and tests. No production activation or matcher transport.
- **Dependencies:** verified Phase 8B/foundations and approved arbitration policy. Production descriptor/activation stays gated by game implementation and 7C; tests use isolated registry fixtures.
- **Acceptance criteria:** cancel-versus-create has one durable outcome; partial writes roll back; stale reservations cannot commit or release newer claims; duplicate/uncertain-response retries preserve one Match; terminal records are immutable and membership private.
- **Required tests and validations:** application/schema/authorization tests, real PostgreSQL two-client races, expiry/fencing/idempotency/failure/grant checks, migration expansion/redeploy/drift/restart checks, frozen install, lint/boundaries/typecheck/build and existing contract compatibility.
- **Status:** Completed

- **Delivered (2026-09-28):** Matches public admission factory/application schemas and transactional repository; user-scoped stable registration receipts, cross-capacity active-ticket uniqueness, immutable intent/deadlines, reservation identity/digest/generation fencing, cancellation during reservation, expiry/release and uncertain-outcome reconciliation. Serializable transactions with bounded retries atomically create the pinned-version CREATED Match, deterministic seats, terminal assignments and one credential-free v1 outbox intent with optional validated correlation. Registration receipts remain readable during maintenance after current user eligibility checks. Production catalog readiness is preserved; executable descriptors exist only as isolated test fixtures until the owning game/registry integration.
- **Persistence / boundaries:** additive seventh migration introduces Matches-owned admission/reservation metadata with foreign keys, uniqueness, immutable intent/terminal-state guards and column-scoped API updates. Worker has no admission grants. Runtime database scoping and ESLint ownership rules enforce Matches access and reject foreign relation traversal. Added two stable error codes and a versioned internal event schema without changing existing OpenAPI operations. ADR-021, the matchmaking boundary/runbook and database ownership documentation record the approved semantics, deployment overlap and remaining gates.
- **Verification:** frozen-lockfile install; Prisma format/validate/generation; scoped API typecheck/tests, scoped architecture tests, build/lint/format, final `pnpm check`, isolated `pnpm test:auth-http:local`, and final diff review passed. Root gate: 1215 tests (630 API, 212 config, 159 architecture/tooling, 112 validation, 48 Bot, 18 database unit, 15 TypeScript, 14 Worker, 7 infrastructure), 12 workspace typecheck scripts, 11 builds, all five existing OpenAPI compatibility checks and 13-workspace/118-source architecture audit. Unchanged tasks may reuse valid Turbo cache. This subphase adds 26 root-gate cases and 15 integration cases.

New cases include eight-request/two-client duplicate registration/creation, competing capacity intents, 20 repeated cancel-versus-commit races, release-versus-commit, exact TTL fencing and stale release, ticket expiry, maintenance receipt recovery, inactive/foreign identities, rollback after an injected outbox failure, lost post-commit response recovery, and role/immutability checks. Migration deploy/redeploy, populated old-schema preservation, zero Prisma-visible drift and admission/reservation/Match/seat/outbox persistence across real PostgreSQL restart pass. Existing Redis outage/recovery checks are regressions, not Phase 9B queue-loss evidence.

Temporary environment reset removed prior logs/binaries, so final verification was rerun. PostgreSQL 18.6 distribution packages were downloaded, checksum-verified against package metadata and extracted into a task-specific temporary directory; a stalled HTTP mirror download was replaced with the official HTTPS archive. No Redis matcher, admission token, distributed matchmaking limiter, Socket transport, outbox dispatcher, production composition, game activation or gameplay lifecycle is claimed. These belong to 9B and later owning phases. Final maintenance/eligibility ordering and executable descriptor integration remain pre-activation gates with 7C. No production migration, commit, push, deployment or live Telegram change. ### Phase 9B — Redis matchmaking orchestration and admission credentials

- **Objective:** connect ephemeral FIFO matchmaking to the durable arbitration port without losing safety on cancellation or store failure.
- **Scope:** queue partitions by game/mode/capacity, atomic Lua enqueue/cancel/claim, unique active tickets, TTL reservation coordination, explicit distributed rate limits, DB commit/release compensation, durable reconciliation after uncertain responses/Redis loss, short-lived session-bound admission tokens and post-commit notification handoff. No Socket or outbox dispatcher implementation.
- **Expected deliverables:** Matchmaking application/Redis adapters, token issuance/validation, versioned contracts, recovery/runbook documentation and integration tests.
- **Dependencies:** completed 9A and explicit authorization to proceed; current Users/Games admission checks. Production readiness remains subject to 7C and executable descriptor integration.
- **Acceptance criteria:** FIFO selection within partition; duplicate enqueue and cancel-versus-match have one logical outcome; match-found requires DB commit; stale/foreign/expired admission credentials fail; Redis loss cannot erase or duplicate committed assignment.
- **Required tests and validations:** real Redis/PostgreSQL races, duplicate enqueue, reservation expiry/release, Redis outage/restart loss, ambiguous DB commit, token tamper/expiry/identity checks, contracts, lint/boundaries/typecheck/build.
- **Status:** Completed

- **Authorization / implementation (2026-09-28):** owner approved continuing to 9B after the 9A gate. Re-read all four required sources using the documented canonical architecture path and inspected the clean committed 9A worktree on `main` at `330f986`. Verified the relevant public arbitration/session ports, Redis ACL/client conventions and existing integration harness; no completed prerequisite needed reimplementation. Implemented bounded FIFO coordination, durable recovery, session-bound credentials and current membership checks. No production activation, transport, dispatcher or later phase is included.

- **Delivered:** Matchmaking application ports and strict v1 command/additive receiver schemas; bounded Lua FIFO queues partitioned by game/mode/capacity, generation-aware duplicate enqueue/cancellation cleanup, recoverable TTL claims and shared per-user search/read/cancel budgets. A trusted bounded tick reserves/commits/releases through Matches only; uncertain responses are durably reconciled before Redis compensation. Queue loss is recovered through stable command receipts, without losing or duplicating committed assignments. No background process or transport starts automatically.
- **Admission / security:** dedicated-purpose rotatable local HS256 credentials bind user/session/ticket/Match, expire within an explicitly configured 1–300 seconds and never outlive the session. Current session and Matches-owned HUMAN membership/admissible lifecycle checks are required for issue/use. Credentials stay redacted and absent from Redis, PostgreSQL, outbox and telemetry. Redis failure denies new search but preserves durable authenticated cancellation/status/admission recovery; counters report rate degradation. Added only API Redis `+time` for lease time; Worker ACL and database grants/schema are unchanged. The existing token-free outbox remains the post-commit handoff. ADR-021 and the matchmaking runbook document these integration details and rollback/recovery boundaries.
- **Verification:** frozen-lockfile install, scoped API typecheck/unit tests, formatting/lint, full `pnpm check`, isolated `pnpm test:auth-http:local` and final diff review pass. Root gate: 1,250 tests (665 API, 212 config, 159 architecture/tooling, 112 validation, 48 Bot, 18 database, 15 TypeScript, 14 Worker, seven infrastructure), 12 workspace typecheck scripts, 11 builds, all five existing OpenAPI checks and 13-workspace/122-source architecture audit. Unchanged tasks may reuse valid Turbo cache. Added 35 unit/application/credential tests and 12 real integration cases; existing infrastructure ACL assertions and admission fixtures are extended.
- **Integration evidence:** all 141 isolated tests pass (16 database, 119 API, six Worker). Coverage includes eight concurrent duplicate searches across instances, FIFO/capacity isolation, 12 repeated cancel-versus-match races, abandoned claims and exact expiry, stale enqueue/cleanup fencing, lost commit responses, unavailable arbitration, current session/revocation/seat checks, distributed quota exhaustion/TTL, complete queue loss during a durable reservation, and actual Redis stop/restart with successful durable cancellation/committed admission recovery. Migration deploy/redeploy, prior-schema preservation, zero Prisma-visible drift and durable PostgreSQL restart regressions pass. Production catalog activation was not enabled.
- **Corrections / environment:** corrected contextual typing of the frozen credential codec, added the read-only membership method to test fixtures, and fixed generation precedence during delayed Redis cleanup. The first integration runs exposed the new Lua lease script's denied TIME command; adding API-only `+time` resolved it, without broadening dangerous commands or key scopes. A temporary tool-environment reset removed earlier processes/logs/binaries, so final evidence was rerun using checksum-verified PostgreSQL 18.6 packages extracted into a task-specific temporary directory and the pinned Docker Redis image. Temporary diagnostics were removed. Harness-owned services/data/credentials were cleaned; no system packages were installed.
- **Limits / mandatory stop:** Phase 9 is complete as an application boundary with local evidence, not a playable or deployed service. No dependency/lockfile change, database migration, live operator control, production values/key provisioning, matcher scheduler/runtime activation, Socket wiring, outbox dispatcher, engine or UI is introduced. Redis loss discards FIFO order; clients retry stable commands to rebuild pending tickets, and terminal receipts have no new deletion policy. Final maintenance/eligibility integration, executable descriptors and 7C remain activation gates. No commit, push, deployment, production data change or live Telegram change was performed. - **Decisions:** [ADR-022](adr/022-coup-module-version-and-policies.md) records offline version isolation, explicit configuration with no production timeout defaults, deterministic timer/bot policies, no deadline reset/reclaim on reconnect, and compatibility behavior. Current snapshots/events validate through identity migration/upcasting; old versions replay with their retained engines and cross-version migration fails with `INCOMPATIBLE_GAME_VERSION`, without inventing timestamps or altering pinned rules. The frontend artifact is an adapter contract, not a React renderer. OD-004 baseline and OD-008 remain unchanged; no activation decision was made.
- **Verification:** frozen-lockfile installation; scoped Game Core build and Coup typecheck/test/build; targeted formatting; lint; architecture checks; full `pnpm check`; and `git diff --check` passed. Coup has 79 passing tests (30 new), including 50 mixed action/timeout games across 2–6 players, 20 differential games against claims-v1, static replay fixtures for state schemas 2/3/4, every-phase player/spectator goldens, hidden-state noninterference, bot-valid commands, deadline/race guards and corrupt restoration/replay rejection. Root gate: 1,332 passing tests including infra, 11 builds, typechecks, all five existing API contract checks, and 13-workspace/132-source architecture audit; unchanged tasks may use Turbo cache. Development schema-literal/fixture typing, a fixture-generation target typo and an expired-window test-driver assumption were corrected before the final passing run.
- **Limits / mandatory stop:** no dependency/lockfile change, database migration, application command transaction, Worker job, Socket/UI implementation, catalog activation, production duration choice, commit, push, deploy or live Telegram change. Production CSPRNG composition, atomic Match persistence/idempotency, queue orchestration and authenticated bot/takeover integration remain their owning phases. Service-backed integration and staging E2E were not applicable to this pure library change; local verification is not live gameplay or launch readiness. Stop at 10D; Phase 11A requires separate authorization.

### Phase 11A — Match lifecycle and atomic command processing

- **Objective:** make matches durable, single-writer, version-checked, and idempotent.
- **Scope:** lifecycle transitions, pinned engine/state/ruleset versions, match players, row lock, `expectedVersion`, `(match_id, action_id)` processing, snapshot/events, and cached retry result in one short transaction.
- **Expected deliverables:** Matches domain/application service, repository implementation, schema evolution, and transaction tests.
- **Dependencies:** Phase 10D and Phase 4B.
- **Acceptance criteria:** accepted commands have at-most-once effect; state/event/version remain atomic; no network I/O occurs in the transaction.
- **Required tests and validations:** lifecycle/domain tests, duplicate/stale/concurrent command integration tests, rollback/failure tests, deterministic replay, lint/typecheck/build.
- **Status:** Completed

- **Authorization / prerequisites (2026-09-29):** owner authorized only 11A and confirmed continuation. Started from clean `main` at `6834457` (Phase 10D). Read the named reviewed architecture in `/home/mehdi/Downloads`, AGENTS, complete plan and execution protocol; the maintained architecture differs only in asset paths and the approved ADR-019 REST amendment. Inspected relevant manifests/task graph, database ownership/schema and existing Matches admission plus complete Coup interfaces. All 79 prerequisite Coup tests passed; no prior phase required repair or reimplementation.
- **Delivered:** Matches lifecycle/application ports, strict v1 readiness/command/trusted lifecycle envelopes, pure lifecycle transition guard, synchronous generic game adapter and composition-only complete Coup integration with server CSPRNG. Durable readiness, recoverable STARTING, atomic initialization, engine-driven FINISHED and trusted TERMINATED transitions operate on existing CREATED records. Engine/state/ruleset pins remain immutable; restored snapshots must match the durable roster, and active commands use persisted rules even after adapter configuration changes.
- **Atomicity / privacy:** Matches-only parameterized `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`, expectedVersion validation, actor/intent-bound `(match_id, action_id)` receipts and minimal cached acknowledgements. Lifecycle/seat changes, snapshot, ordered audit events, receipt and state-free `MATCH_CHANGED_V1` outbox intent commit together, with bounded transaction-conflict retries and no external network calls/publishing inside the transaction. Restricted initialization events permit replay without independent RNG-material storage. Additive nullable ready/digest metadata and trusted lifecycle actors preserve old insert shapes; legacy digest-less receipts fail closed. [ADR-023](adr/023-match-atomic-processing.md) and [Match processing](architecture/match-processing.md) record semantics and rollout/rollback limits.
- **Verification:** frozen-lockfile install (including after recording two existing workspace dependency edges); Prisma format/validate/generation/build; scoped API typecheck/test/build; targeted formatting; lint; architecture checks; complete `pnpm check`; isolated `pnpm test:auth-http:local`; and diff review passed. Root gate: 1,347 tests including infrastructure, 12 workspace typecheck scripts, 11 builds, five unchanged OpenAPI compatibility checks and 13-workspace/136-source architecture audit. Unchanged tasks may use Turbo cache. Added 13 API unit/application/adapter tests, one database boundary/migration test and one additive shared-error case.
- **Integration evidence:** 155 isolated PostgreSQL/Redis tests pass (16 database, 133 API, six Worker), including 14 new Match cases. Eight-request/two-client duplicates, distinct competing commands, ready/activation/termination races, stale/changed/foreign intents, post-commit retry after service reconstruction, bot-seat denial, unknown pins/corrupt state, rollback at each write stage, closed-database failure and complete persisted Coup replay/result equality pass. Migration deploy/redeploy, zero Prisma-visible drift and previous account/session/room expansion regressions pass. Real PostgreSQL restart comparison now includes Match snapshots, seats, game events, processed commands and outbox. Existing admission/matchmaking/Redis outage tests remain green.
- **Corrections / environment:** corrected JSON type naming, nullable/UUID test-fixture typing, an out-of-range synthetic rule value and test commands using `INCOME` instead of the established `DECLARE_INCOME` intent. Final runs have zero failures. PostgreSQL 18.6 Ubuntu packages were checksum-verified against local package metadata and extracted into a phase-specific temporary directory without system installation. Harness-owned clusters, Redis containers and temporary credentials were cleaned; downloaded binary cache remains reusable.
- **Limits / mandatory stop:** 11A is complete as an application boundary, not a live playable service. Only workspace dependency edges changed; no dependency versions, production duration/rules values, grants, live migration, commit, push, deploy or Telegram settings changed. No outbox dispatcher/result projection, Socket/resync transport, matcher scheduler, private-room-to-Match orchestration, timer/reconnect/takeover jobs, admin route or UI was added. These factories are opt-in; catalog/7C activation and OD-008 remain gated. Staging gameplay E2E has no configured environment, executable transport/UI or declared E2E script; local verification is not load/soak/network-partition or launch-readiness evidence. Stop after 11A; 11B requires separate authorization.

### Phase 11B — Transactional outbox and result projection inputs

- **Objective:** durably bridge committed match changes to realtime, jobs, and stats.
- **Scope:** versioned outbox events, claim/publish marking, retry/backoff, consumer deduplication, finish/result events, and trace-context propagation.
- **Expected deliverables:** outbox repository/dispatcher in Worker, consumer idempotency store/pattern, and integration tests.
- **Dependencies:** Phase 11A.
- **Acceptance criteria:** outbox row is written with state/events; nothing publishes before commit; retries may redeliver but cannot duplicate logical side effects.
- **Required tests and validations:** commit/rollback tests, dispatcher retry and duplicate-delivery tests, failed-queue behavior, lint/typecheck/build.
- **Status:** Completed

- **Authorization / prerequisites (2026-09-29):** owner explicitly approved 11B after the 11A gate. Verified only the existing atomic writer, outbox/receipt schema, ownership/grants and Worker/BullMQ composition. No blocking prerequisite or earlier-phase reimplementation was needed.
- **Delivered:** Matches-owned outbox application/repository port exposed through `@hambazi/api/matches`; Worker polling and versioned BullMQ publication after claim commit; SKIP LOCKED bounded batches, expiring leases, monotonic attempt fencing, retry/backoff, quarantine and trusted same-event-ID redrive. Optional explicit configuration leaves dispatch disabled by default. Shutdown drains claims before closing publisher/database. The existing `matchmaking.found` v1 intent remains compatible.
- **Atomic result / correlation:** finishing a Match now inserts strict `MATCH_FINISHED_V1` alongside its snapshot, audit events, receipt and change intent. Standard results must match the roster; finish-outbox failure rolls back the complete command and retry produces one result input. Validated trusted requestId/traceparent travels separately from player intent through transaction, outbox and job/consumer envelope. Telemetry projects only allowed identifiers/counters, never payload/state/raw errors.
- **Persistence / consumer pattern:** additive nullable `claimed_until` migration and a single additional Worker delivery-column grant; fixed transaction-only outbox locking, no arbitrary SQL surface. Existing consumer receipts and owned effects commit atomically via the leaderboard-scoped `consumeOnce` primitive. Concurrent duplicates skip effects; failures roll back receipt/effect. [ADR-024](adr/024-transactional-outbox-delivery.md) and the [outbox runbook](architecture/match-outbox.md) document contracts, migration, recovery, rollout and rollback.
- **Verification:** frozen-lockfile install; Prisma validation/generation; scoped API/Worker typechecks and API/Worker/Config/Database tests; targeted formatting; lint and architecture audit; complete `pnpm check`; isolated `pnpm test:auth-http:local`; and final diff/format review pass. Nine migrations deploy/redeploy, populated legacy outbox expansion, zero Prisma-visible drift and real PostgreSQL restart comparisons including consumer receipts pass. Queue unavailability is injected at the publisher boundary; queue identity/replay uses real BullMQ/Redis. This is not a production Redis-loss drill.

Realtime fanout/resync, gameplay jobs, result-table/statistics projection and automated incident detection remain pending. Trusted per-ID redrive preserves event IDs and fences; retained failed BullMQ jobs need owning-consumer retry after repair. No historical finish backfill, retention policy, production retry values, dependency version change, enabled deployment, live migration, commit, push or Telegram change. ### Phase 12A — Versioned realtime contracts and authenticated gateway

### Phase 12B — Ordered delivery, fanout, and resynchronization base

- **Objective:** implement at-least-once player delivery with per-match sequence handling.
- **Scope:** post-commit fanout, player-specific projections, Redis adapter behavior, monotonic `seq`, duplicate/gap handling contract, snapshot/event catch-up endpoints, and connection draining.
- **Expected deliverables:** fanout adapters, resync use cases/contracts, client sequence utility, and multi-node/failure tests.
- **Dependencies:** Phase 12A.
- **Acceptance criteria:** only sanitized views leave the server; duplicates are harmless; gaps trigger catch-up; snapshots expose `stateVersion` and `lastEventSeq` and replace only older client state.
- **Required tests and validations:** delivery/ordering/gap tests, multi-socket integration, Redis-adapter degradation test, drain test, lint/typecheck/build.
- **Status:** Completed

- **Implemented (2026-09-29):** Matches-owned snapshot/catch-up ports read a consistent roster, role, pinned descriptor, safe view and event checkpoint under the existing writer lock. Coup projects and schema-validates PlayerView/SpectatorView; replaced humans receive spectator views. Public invalidations expose only durable IDs, seq and originating stateVersion, with no internal event type/payload. Catch-up is bounded to 100 events with current-snapshot fallback. No migration or grant change was required.
- **Delivery:** opt-in API BullMQ consumer owns only `match-changed-v1`. State-free committed outbox wakeups fan out through the Socket.IO static sharded Redis adapter; each node reauthenticates its own sockets and obtains recipient-specific projections. Socket work is serialized, snapshot acknowledgements advance per-socket cursors, and missing acknowledgements fail publication for retry/reconnect. Redis connection/subscription failure latches readiness false and closes transports. Drain waits for in-flight operations; API shutdown now drains realtime before HTTP. Production composition remains disabled.
- **Contracts / decisions:** added snapshot/catch-up intents, snapshot/invalidation payload schemas, generated catalog and an additional frozen delivery baseline while preserving the 12A baseline. Shared `createMatchSequence` handles duplicate/gap detection and rejects older/equal snapshots. [ADR-026](adr/026-realtime-delivery-and-resynchronization.md) records snapshot-based rendering, ephemeral cross-node wakeups, retry semantics, private Redis channels, fail-closed recovery and composition order. No session, retention, CCU or production rules decision was resolved.
- **Verification:** frozen-lockfile installation, scoped API/Contracts builds, API typecheck/tests, contract generation/check, lint, formatting, architecture checks, `pnpm check`, `pnpm build --force`, production dependency audit and final diff review pass. The forced build ran all 11 builds without cache; unchanged checks may otherwise use Turbo cache. Nine unit/transport cases and two integration cases were added.
- **Integration evidence:** all 167 isolated PostgreSQL/Redis tests pass (17 Database, 136 API, 14 Worker). New cases verify durable event/version mapping, safe per-player projections, denied foreign membership, spectator downgrade, incompatible versions, catch-up fallbacks, two real Socket.IO nodes with Redis, real BullMQ consumption, node-specific projections, Redis-adapter connection loss, healthy-node continuation and cleanup. Unit/transport tests cover duplicate/gap/stale snapshot handling, multiple sockets, revocation, membership loss, projection failure, invalid/missing acknowledgements, invalid/wrong-owner jobs, retries and an in-flight-command drain. Existing migrations/restart and outbox regressions pass through the declared harness.

Concurrent verification initially collided during Prisma generation; gates were then serialized and all builds forced once to refresh evidence. Fixed test-only readonly/socket typing and adapter declaration-export errors. The first Redis adapter variant could not establish pattern subscriptions under the existing ACL; static sharded subscriptions pass with the unchanged private ACL. Final runs have zero failures.
- **Limits / mandatory stop:** no production activation, database migration, deployment, commit or push. Redis loss is exercised by dropping a real adapter connection, not a production partition/load drill. The two-node transport uses controlled application ports; durable Match projection is verified separately against PostgreSQL. The client sequence utility is ready for later Mini App integration; there is no declared gameplay E2E script or staging environment. Read-lock contention, capacity, reconnect-storm and Telegram/proxy validation remain later gates. Phase 7C remains mandatory before live activation. ### Phase 13 — Reconnection, timeout, and Bot takeover

- **Objective:** preserve active matches across disconnects and safely replace expired seats.
- **Scope:** presence, grace deadline, BullMQ delayed jobs, `connection_epoch`, expected state/version guards, reconnect cancellation/resync, `REPLACED_BY_BOT`, and spectator-only human return.
- **Expected deliverables:** Presence/reconnect application services, versioned job payload/handler, bot command integration, and race tests.
- **Dependencies:** Phase 12B and Phase 10D.
- **Acceptance criteria:** stale/duplicate jobs are no-ops; reconnect-vs-timeout has one valid winner; post-takeover humans receive only SpectatorView under the baseline policy.
- **Required tests and validations:** timer/integration/E2E tests, reconnect-vs-timeout races, duplicate job tests, worker restart/retry tests, hidden-information checks, lint/typecheck/build.
- **Status:** Completed

- **Authorization / prerequisite assessment (2026-09-29):** owner authorized Phase 13 only. Started on clean `main` at `41cbb37` (Phase 12B). Reviewed all required sources, using the documented canonical architecture copy and comparing the named original in `/home/mehdi/Downloads`; differences are asset paths and the already approved ADR-019 amendment. Inspected manifests/workspace/task graph, the Match writer/resync ports, Coup timer/bot/reconnect policies, Socket subscription/disconnect handling and seat schema. No defective or missing earlier-phase prerequisite was identified; no earlier phase was redone.
- **Historical decision blocker (resolved by owner approval below):** the existing gateway allows multiple simultaneous sockets for one user, and `gateway.test.ts` explicitly verifies independent delivery to multiple sockets. Its disconnect callback currently only clears the session-expiry timer. `match_players` has a seat-level epoch/status/disconnected timestamp, but no per-connection lease or durable grace deadline. Architecture section 12.4 specifies epoch/version fencing and no reclaim, while ADR-022 and ADR-026 leave presence implementation to this phase. None selects whether all healthy subscribed sockets or only the newest connection preserve human control, or how a crashed node's missing disconnect callback becomes an authoritative timeout. These choices affect visible seat loss and persistence; the execution protocol's Handling Ambiguity section requires an owner decision before implementing them. This is a Phase 13 policy gate, not an earlier-phase defect.
- **Proposed policy — subsequently approved:** any healthy authenticated, Match-subscribed socket keeps the human seat connected across API nodes. Closing one of several sockets does not start grace. Last-socket disconnect starts grace; lease expiry detects lost processes even when disconnect callbacks never execute. Use minimal Matches-owned durable lease/deadline/fencing metadata for takeover correctness, with Redis retaining ephemeral socket presence and lobby projections. Store no token, raw handshake or game payload in lease metadata. All authority changes serialize under the existing Match lock; Redis reads, queue operations and Socket I/O remain outside the transaction. Runtime lease/heartbeat/grace durations are explicit validated inputs, with no production defaults selected here.
- **Recovery and retained requirements in the proposal:** reconnect invalidates old epochs and resynchronizes; queue cancellation remains best effort. A timeout must recheck durable deadline, connection epoch, Match version and human seat status atomically. Stale/duplicate jobs do not mutate; reconciliation schedules fresh guarded jobs against the original grace deadline after intervening Match writes, worker restart or Redis loss. Reconciliation must not renew grace merely because the queue was rebuilt. Engine deadlines continue unchanged; takeover remains irreversible for that Match, and returning humans receive only SpectatorView. Bots receive only PlayerView and use the existing versioned policy. Production activation remains disabled and Phase 7C stays mandatory.
- **Alternative for the focused decision:** only the newest connection controls liveness; older connections cannot keep the seat alive. This reduces connection aggregation, but losing the newest connection can start grace even while an older tab/device remains connected. Do not silently introduce this user-visible restriction under the existing multi-socket delivery contract.
- **Assessment validation:** `pnpm --filter @hambazi/coup-engine test` passes 79/79 tests across five files; `pnpm --filter @hambazi/api test` passes 704/704 across 29 files, including the existing Match/resync and real loopback Socket suites. `pnpm format:check` and `git diff --check` pass; diff/status review confirms this plan is the sole changed file. These are prerequisite regression results, not Phase 13 implementation or service-backed race evidence. No dependency install, typecheck/build, database/Redis integration, migration or E2E run is claimed for this documentation-only assessment.
- **Historical assessment-only result / stop:** only this plan changes. No product source, contract, migration, grant, dependency, accepted ADR, live service, commit, push or deployment changes. Resume Phase 13 after the owner selects the connection-liveness policy; record the approved design in an ADR before implementation. Do not start Phase 14A.

- **Owner approval / implementation split (2026-09-29):** owner explicitly approved aggregate multi-socket liveness, last-valid-socket grace, lease expiry for crashed nodes and irreversible spectator-only return. [ADR-027](adr/027-match-connection-leases.md) records acceptance. Split implementation into 13A (durable connection/seat transaction foundation) and 13B (Presence/gateway, BullMQ recovery and automated timer/bot execution) so the authority/persistence change is reviewed separately from transport/queue activation. Complete only 13A in this interaction and stop at its mandatory subphase gate. No earlier-phase prerequisite repair is needed.

### Phase 13A — Durable connection leases and takeover transactions

- **Objective:** establish the durable authority for aggregate liveness and reconnect-versus-takeover decisions.
- **Scope:** Matches-owned per-connection leases, pinned explicit policy, last-lease grace deadline, session-expiry bounds, epoch/version guards, trusted reconciliation/timeout ports, atomic audit/checkpoints/outbox, irreversible Bot replacement and existing spectator resync.
- **Expected deliverables:** additive lease/deadline migration, least-privilege grants and ownership enforcement, server-only application factory, versioned timeout schema, ADR-027 and PostgreSQL race/privacy/rollback tests.
- **Dependencies:** completed 10D/12B and the approved Phase 13 connection policy.
- **Acceptance criteria:** one live socket preserves the seat; closed/expired identities cannot resurrect; heartbeats do not advance gameplay versions; stale/duplicate jobs do not mutate; grace survives restart/version changes; reconnect-versus-timeout has one winner; takeover preserves engine state and denies human commands/private views.
- **Required tests and validations:** schema/error tests, two-client concurrency/idempotency tests, node-crash/expiry/reconstruction cases, rollback, least privilege, migration/restart/drift, hidden-information checks, frozen install, lint/boundaries/typecheck/build and existing contract gates.
- **Status:** Completed

- **Delivered (2026-09-29):** `createMatchReconnectPort` exposes trusted connect/renew/disconnect/reconcile/timeout operations with strict inputs, safe errors and validated correlation. Matches owns minimal per-connection lease metadata and a pinned explicit policy. New sockets advance epoch; duplicate connect does not extend expiry; heartbeat renewal preserves gameplay version/deadlines and is capped by authenticated session expiry. One surviving lease keeps the seat connected. Expired/closed identities cannot resurrect, and tombstones have no unapproved deletion policy.
- **Atomicity / recovery:** all operations use the existing Match writer lock. Last-lease loss persists the original grace deadline; crash/reconstruction and gameplay-version changes do not extend it. Stale/early/duplicate timeouts are no-ops. Reconnect versus timeout has one committed winner; takeover atomically records BOT/REPLACED_BY_BOT/replacedAt plus audit event, version/sequence, minimal checkpoint and state-free outbox wakeup. Existing human command denial and exact SpectatorView resync remain authoritative. Engine snapshots/deadlines are unchanged.
- **Persistence / boundaries:** tenth additive migration adds nullable policy/deadline fields and Matches-owned lease storage, immutable connection identity/closed tombstones and pinned policy. API receives narrow lease create/read/expiry/closure privileges; Worker is read-only on leases and uses existing Matches-owned seat writes for reconcile/takeover. Runtime and ESLint ownership checks prevent foreign-module access. No public wire, dependency or lockfile change. [ADR-027](adr/027-match-connection-leases.md) and the [reconnect boundary/runbook](architecture/match-reconnect.md) document authority, rollout/rollback and the 13B integration obligations.
- **Verification:** frozen-lockfile install, Prisma format/validate/generation/build, scoped API typecheck/tests (711), formatting/lint, architecture checks (13 workspaces/154 source files), all workspace typechecks, 11 builds, six API contract checks and final diff review pass. Added nine unit/ownership cases and 15 integration cases. All 1,389 workspace tests pass with the explicit `pnpm exec turbo run test --concurrency=1 -- --testTimeout=15000`; seven infrastructure tests also pass, totaling 1,396. That final serial workspace run executed all 29 prerequisite/test tasks without cache; separate build/typecheck runs may use valid Turbo cache.
- **Default-gate limitation / corrections:** unmodified `pnpm check` passes formatting/lint/boundaries/typechecks but fails the unchanged Coup generated-model case at its default five-second timeout on this environment. Serial task execution and a separate single-worker probe still exceeded that limit. With the explicit 15-second runner limit the case completes in approximately 6.3 seconds and all 79 Coup tests pass. No source test limit, assertion or engine implementation was changed, and a green default `pnpm check` is not claimed. A nullable closure typing error was corrected before scoped typecheck passed. A temporary tool reset removed the first verification processes/logs/binaries; final verification was restarted using checksum-verified PostgreSQL 18.6 packages extracted into a user cache without system installation.
- **Integration evidence:** `pnpm test:auth-http:local` passes 182 isolated PostgreSQL/Redis tests (17 Database, 151 API, 14 Worker). The 15 new cases cover independent clients, multiple sockets, renewals and session bounds, missing disconnect callbacks, duplicate/reconstructed operations, 12 repeated reconnect-vs-timeout races, stale epochs/versions, original-deadline recovery, rollback on outbox failure, immutable policy/tombstones, least privilege, correlated Worker takeover, unavailable storage, spectator privacy and catch-up checkpoints. All ten migrations deploy/redeploy, Prisma-visible zero drift, populated legacy expansion and real PostgreSQL restart comparisons including lease rows pass. Harness-owned clusters/containers/data are cleaned; downloaded tools remain reusable.
- **Limits / mandatory stop:** this is a dormant server application boundary, not an automated playable resilience flow. No Presence/gateway heartbeat wiring, BullMQ delayed-job/retry/recovery loop, bot/timer command execution, production activation, live migration, commit, push or deployment. Those runtime integrations and their actual crash/queue-restart tests remain 13B; existing Worker regressions are not new delayed-job evidence. No declared gameplay E2E script or staging environment is available. Durable heartbeat write cost and tombstone growth remain capacity/retention considerations. Parent Phase 13 remains In Progress. Stop at the 13A gate before 13B.

### Phase 13B — Presence transport, delayed jobs, recovery and bot execution

- **Objective:** automate the approved durable policy across Socket nodes and Worker restarts.
- **Scope:** authenticated Match subscription/heartbeat/disconnect orchestration through Presence and Matches public ports; resync and best-effort cancellation; versioned BullMQ delayed jobs with bounded retry/drain; paginated recovery of lost jobs and expired leases; generic trusted engine timer/bot commands and PlayerView-only Coup policy integration.
- **Expected deliverables:** Presence ports, gateway wiring and additive contracts where needed, Worker scheduler/handlers, pinned game adapter extensions, bounded recovery/runbook and cross-node/job-restart integration tests.
- **Dependencies:** completed 13A, existing 10D policies and 12B resync/outbox delivery. Explicit runtime settings remain required; production activation remains gated by 7C.
- **Acceptance criteria:** all healthy sockets contribute; no lost disconnect after node death can stall a Match indefinitely; stale jobs are harmless; queue reconstruction preserves original deadlines; bots use only PlayerView; human return after takeover stays spectator-only; no gateway/Worker-owned game rules.
- **Required tests and validations:** real Socket/PostgreSQL/Redis/BullMQ multi-node and restart/retry tests, reconnect-vs-timeout/command races, full bot/timeout progression and replay, hidden-information tests, contract compatibility, lint/typecheck/build and staging E2E when available.
- **Status:** Completed

- **Authorization (2026-09-30):** owner approved 13B. Starting from clean `a9cc41b` (Phase 13A); implementing transport, recovery and trusted automation only.

- **Delivered (2026-09-30):** Presence owns per-transport subscription/lease orchestration through public Matches ports. The gateway reauthenticates received Engine.IO pongs and existing presence pings, registers pre-activation subscriptions on a subsequent healthy heartbeat, resynchronizes using the existing acknowledged snapshot contract, reports the projected role in subscription acknowledgement, and drains disconnect cleanup after accepted socket work. Independent healthy sockets preserve the seat; actual killed API processes leave leases for expiry recovery. Returning replaced users remain spectators. Optional cancellation is fire-and-forget and cannot block resync/heartbeats.
- **Trusted automation:** Matches exposes bounded cursor recovery, state-free planning and guarded execution. Bot/timer work reuses the atomic versioned writer, replay events, minimal checkpoints, result mapping and outbox. Bot seat/epoch and Match version are checked under the lock; pinned adapters guard engine revision/deadline. Coup uses only PlayerView for policy decisions, gives overdue timers precedence, and treats a bot job crossing its deadline as a no-op. Stale/duplicate work performs no writes. Adapter-version errors retain safe stable codes.
- **Worker / recovery:** separate version-1 BullMQ disconnect/automation jobs avoid competing with realtime outbox delivery. Stable descriptor hashes deduplicate producers; validated optional correlation flows into committed outbox events. Explicit retry/backoff, one-page-per-tick recovery, original deadlines, failure isolation, queue reconstruction and an explicit application drain deadline are implemented. Shutdown closes queue connections even after a failed drain; the owning runtime closes its database pool. Worker composition remains opt-in with explicitly supplied pinned games/policy/settings. No production defaults, schema/grant/dependency/lockfile or wire-schema changes.
- **Verification:** frozen-lockfile install, formatting, lint, architecture audit (13 workspaces/158 source files), all relevant workspace typechecks, 11 builds, six API contract checks and diff review pass. `pnpm exec turbo run test --concurrency=1 -- --testTimeout=15000` passes 1,410 workspace tests (726 API, 28 Worker); `pnpm test:infra` passes seven more, totaling 1,417. Added 21 unit/transport cases. Final Turbo runs reuse valid caches for unchanged work; modified API/Worker code was rebuilt/typechecked and its suites rerun through prerequisite tasks.
- **Integration evidence:** final `pnpm test:auth-http:local` passes 192 isolated tests (17 Database, 157 API, 18 Worker), including ten new cases. Coverage includes two real API child processes with SIGKILL and a surviving healthy socket, last-crashed-lease deadline reconstruction, automated persisted Coup games with exact replay and one finish outbox, concurrent human/automated writers, stale bot epochs, bounded cursor scans, safe adapter/input rejection, actual BullMQ full bot progression, lost-queue reconstruction across Worker recreation, retry/deduplication/cancellation and bounded drain of blocked application work. Existing ten migrations, redeploy/drift and PostgreSQL restart checks pass. Child processes and harness-owned services are cleaned up; no live Telegram credentials were used.
- **Corrections / default-gate limitation:** fixed overdue-bot versus engine-timeout ordering found by PostgreSQL tests; also corrected initial branch/query typings, test-only compiled-adapter import/decorator typing and public-surface placement before final checks. Unmodified `pnpm check` passes formatting/lint/boundaries/typechecks but again hits the unchanged Coup generated-model test's default five-second timeout. The explicit 15-second runner verification passes; no engine test, assertion or source timeout was changed, and a green default `pnpm check` is not claimed.
- **Current result / mandatory stop:** 13B and parent Phase 13 are complete as locally verified opt-in backend capabilities. [ADR-027](adr/027-match-connection-leases.md) and the [runbook](architecture/match-reconnect.md) document composition, heartbeat constraints, recovery, drain and rollback. No declared gameplay E2E script or staging environment exists; production settings/activation, Phase 7C, capacity/reconnect-storm/soak and retention gates remain open. Recovery latency depends on active Match count and explicit scan settings. No commit, push, deployment or live migration. Stop here; Phase 14A remains unauthorized.

### Phase 14A — Telegram Mini App shell and shared platform UI

Request a separately authorized prerequisite to define and expose registry-owned matchmaking mode metadata through an additive catalog contract, with compatibility tests and documentation, while preserving disabled production admission. ### Phase 14B — Coup frontend adapter and game UI

- **Objective:** render and operate a complete Coup match solely from PlayerView/events.
- **Scope:** table/player/card views, action/target/challenge/block/exchange/lose-card flows, timers, reconnect/gap indicators, spectator state, animation without rule authority, and version mismatch handling.
- **Expected deliverables:** `apps/web/src/games/coup` adapter/screens/store/components and browser tests.
- **Dependencies:** Phase 14A and Phase 10D.
- **Acceptance criteria:** every valid server phase has a usable UI; unsupported versions fail safely; DOM/client state does not contain other players' hidden cards.
- **Required tests and validations:** component/state-adapter tests, golden UI states, hidden-data inspection, responsive/accessibility checks, Playwright match-flow tests, lint/typecheck/build.
- **Status:** Completed

- **Authorization / prerequisites (2026-10-01):** owner explicitly authorized 14B. The reviewed 14A shell and 10D complete Coup descriptor/views/command fixtures were present; no earlier phase was reimplemented. Production admission remains intentionally disabled under the existing 7C activation gate.
- **Delivered:** `apps/web/src/games/coup` now validates the exact versioned public PlayerView/SpectatorView, renders public seats, card backs/reveals, the viewer's private hand, phase/action/claim/loss context, server deadline, winner and spectator state. Every command control is projected from server `legalCommands`, including target, challenge, block, proof, loss and exact exchange selections. Version/role/identity mismatch or extra hidden fields fail closed. Match `stateVersion` and Coup `revision` remain independent as required by lifecycle writes. The in-memory session stores only validated public projections; the client does not import the engine or derive game legality. CSS motion respects reduced-motion preference.
- **Transport / safety:** `game:command` uses schema version 1, stable action ID, Match ID, expected snapshot version and a server-provided command. Ambiguous retries retain the same envelope; conflicts request resync. Invalidation, gap and disconnect discard the old view while catch-up runs; an invalid response or unresolved gap reports a fixed-code error. Spectator return cannot reuse the prior hand. No raw server error or hidden card is logged/persisted.
- **Evidence (2026-10-01):** `pnpm --filter @hambazi/web test` passes 16 tests across three files, including all seven engine golden public phases, independent Match/engine revisions and malformed/hidden-field rejection. `pnpm --filter @hambazi/web test:browser` passes 11 Chromium scenarios, including command envelopes, exchange, challenge/loss, finish, spectator and version failure. The preview isolation test passes (one scenario). Axe reports zero violations on tested action/exchange/finish states after the revealed-card contrast correction. 320px screenshots and enlarged-text reflow were inspected. Web typecheck/build, root lint/format and the architecture audit (13 workspaces, 171 source files) pass; production build's preview-boundary check passes. `git diff --check` passes. An initial concurrent browser/preview run failed only because both fixtures bind port 4174; sequential rerun passed. No frozen install was needed because dependencies/lockfile did not change.
- **Limits / gate:** Browser flows use disposable golden-view fixtures with synthetic transitions, not a running durable engine or live Telegram. No controlled staging HTTPS/WebView environment was available for staged gameplay E2E. The route is gated by both Vite development mode and preview-only HTML metadata; ordinary development leaves it inert, while the production build boundary checks exclude the route marker and fixture metadata.
