### Phase 15A — Text chat

- **Objective:** add bounded, membership-protected match chat without weakening gameplay privacy.
- **Scope:** send/list contracts, PostgreSQL history/pagination, Socket delivery, membership checks, length/rate limits, escaping/sanitization, and telemetry redaction.
- **Expected deliverables:** Chat module, persistence/realtime adapters, Mini App UI, tests.
- **Dependencies:** Phase 14B; final retention/report/mute/age behavior depends on `OD-005`, so only the documented MVP-safe baseline may be implemented before resolution.
- **Acceptance criteria:** non-members cannot read/send; content is not logged/traced; stored/displayed content cannot inject active markup; pagination is stable.
- **Required tests and validations:** validation/auth/rate-limit/XSS tests, repository/realtime integration tests, UI tests, lint/typecheck/build.
- **Status:** Completed

- **Authorization / prerequisite assessment (2026-10-01):** owner authorized only Phase 15A. Started on clean `main` at `9f77b86` (Coup PreviewMode). Read all four required sources in full, including the named reviewed architecture in `/home/mehdi/Downloads`; comparison with the canonical repository copy confirms only asset paths and the approved ADR-019 amendment differ. Inspected the actual manifests, workspace/task graph, Chat scaffold, PostgreSQL ownership/grants/schema, public Match membership port, Socket authentication/delivery/catalog and the completed 14B public-view renderer. No missing or defective earlier-phase prerequisite was found; no earlier phase was reimplemented. Phase 7C remains an intentional activation gate, not a Chat prerequisite defect.
- **Historical in-phase policy gate (resolved by owner approval below):** architecture sections 13.1 and 16.3 require membership-protected, bounded, rate-limited plain-text chat, but do not select the chat permissions of a Bot-replaced human, message/read budgets or retry/history semantics. `apps/api/src/matches/application/membership.ts` exposes only `PLAYER`/`SPECTATOR`; its repository downgrades Bot-replaced humans and denies terminated Matches, but supplies no Chat-specific permission. `apps/api/src/chat/index.ts` is explicitly unimplemented; realtime reserves only the outgoing `chat:message` name, and no Chat runtime policy or accepted Chat ADR exists. The protocol's Handling Ambiguity section requires approval before freezing decisions materially affecting public behavior, security, persistence or contracts. This is a Phase 15A decision gate, not an earlier-phase implementation blocker. OD-005's final retention/report/mute/age decision is due before public beta and is not incorrectly treated as a prerequisite for all offline Chat development.
- **Historical temporary-baseline proposal — accepted below:** authenticated current Match members may read; only `PLAYER` members may send, so Bot-replaced humans have read-only Chat. Recheck session and membership on each intent and recipient delivery; no public spectators, Bot-authored messages or separate spectator channel. Use plain text, NFC normalization, visible nonempty content, Persian/ZWNJ/emoji support and rejection of malformed Unicode/control/bidi-formatting input; never interpret markup or auto-link content. Recommend a 500-code-point message limit, a 50-message maximum page and explicit shared per-user 60-second budgets of 10 sends / 60 history reads across sockets/nodes. Runtime values must be explicit and validated, with no silently enabled production defaults. Exhaustion returns a stable rate-limit error; Redis failure denies Chat send/list, without altering durable gameplay/session behavior. These proposed numbers are provisional abuse/resource controls, not capacity or legal conclusions.
- **Historical persistence / delivery proposal — accepted below:** actor-bound stable command IDs give one stored message for repeated matching sends and a conflict for changed reuse. Chat owns its history and monotonically ordered pagination/catch-up checkpoint independently of gameplay versions/sequences. Commit message, retry identity and content-free durable delivery intent atomically; reuse the generic outbox/queue pattern through reviewed owner surfaces, without giving Chat access to Match state/tables or placing text in Redis jobs/telemetry. Fanout reads authorized content from PostgreSQL after commit, rechecks each recipient and supports duplicate/gap recovery. Bound each history page and the browser's in-memory message buffer; clear content on logout/Match switch and preserve retry identity after an ambiguous send. No auto-deletion duration, report/mute workflow, age rule or public-beta activation is selected; final OD-005 remains unresolved. Retained history/storage growth must remain an explicit pre-beta policy gate rather than an indefinite-retention promise. Record an owner-approved temporary baseline in an ADR before implementing these public contracts.
- **Historical safe partial result:** assessment and this plan update only. No Chat implementation, contract/schema/grant/dependency change, accepted ADR, service startup, migration, commit, push, deployment or live Telegram change. The stale Current gate below is reconciled with the already completed 14B section and the current 15A authorization; this does not redo 14B or authorize 15B.
- **Assessment verification:** `pnpm --filter @hambazi/api test --testNamePattern='match membership authorization'` passes the three relevant membership tests (724 other tests skipped); `pnpm --filter @hambazi/web test --testNamePattern='Coup public view boundary'` passes all four 14B public-view tests (12 other tests skipped). These verify the reused authorization/projection prerequisites only, not Chat behavior. `pnpm format:check`, `git diff --check` and final status/diff review cover the plan-only change. No Chat tests were added. Database/Redis integration, browser Chat E2E, lint/typecheck/build and installation were not run because no product code, contract, dependency or schema changed. Resume the same Phase 15A after the focused temporary-baseline decision; Phase 15B remains Pending and unauthorized.

- **Owner approval / resumption (2026-10-01):** owner explicitly approved the proposed temporary 15A Chat baseline. ADR-029 records the temporary policy before implementation. Resume only 15A; OD-005 remains unresolved and 15B is not authorized.

- **Delivered:** the Chat application/repository and explicit public factory own validation, membership/role decisions, independent sequencing, stable actor-bound send IDs and keyset history. NFC visible plain text supports Persian/ZWNJ/ZWJ/emoji, rejects controls/malformed Unicode and remains escaped text without auto-linking. Runtime settings are explicit and bounded at 500 code points / 50 messages / 10 sends / 60 reads per 60-second shared user window; smaller budgets are supported. Redis failure denies sends/history without deleting durable data. Spectators read but cannot send, including Bot-replaced members; every intent/delivery uses the public Match membership port.
- **Persistence / transport:** additive `202610010001_chat` migration retains old insert shapes, adds nullable command/sequence metadata and uniqueness, and allocates sequences through a separate stream. Message, sequence and content-free outbox commit atomically with bounded serializable retries; Chat cannot query Match state or claim/read/update outbox. API column grants and runtime/ESLint scoping preserve ownership; Worker cannot read chat content. The shared outbox accepts an injected owner decoder, with the existing Match decoder as default. Worker composition uses public Match/Chat schemas and queues only IDs/sequence/correlation. Opt-in Socket send/list and two-node wakeups reauthenticate/recheck each recipient and fetch PostgreSQL text for direct local delivery. Missing acknowledgements fail/retry and close the affected local socket under the existing gateway policy; reconnect/history repairs missed deliveries. No game stateVersion or event sequence changes.
- **Mini App / contracts:** the new Match chat UI renders plain text, reports read-only/reconnect/rate/error states, supports earlier history, detects duplicates/gaps and repairs missed wakeups. Memory is bounded to 200 messages and disposed on Match switch/logout; draft/retry identity is memory-only. Stable send IDs survive ambiguous commit-before-ack failures. History responses overtaken by live delivery cannot regress the cursor. Game snapshot invalidation does not clear/reload Chat, and disposed screens reject late send results. New additive chat contracts and `CHAT_CONFLICT` have a generated event catalog and new frozen Chat baseline; previous REST/realtime baselines are unchanged. [ADR-029](adr/029-temporary-match-chat.md) and [Match chat](architecture/match-chat.md) record temporary policy, ownership, rollout and rollback.
- **Verification (2026-10-01):** `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` passes with unchanged dependencies/lockfile. Scoped API unit/contract tests pass 745 cases; Web passes 22, Worker 29, Config 218, Database 22 and ESLint 161. `pnpm exec turbo run test --concurrency=1 -- --testTimeout=15000` passes all 1,456 workspace tests through 30 uncached prerequisite/test tasks; `pnpm test:infra` passes seven more. The explicit 15-second runner preserves the already recorded default Coup model-test timeout gap; this phase does not claim a green default `pnpm check` or change the engine test. Root lint, formatting, architecture audit (13 workspaces / 180 source files), all workspace typechecks (19 tasks), all 11 builds and all six API contract checks pass. Prisma formatting/generation and contract generation pass; `git diff --check` passes.
- **Integration / browser evidence:** the declared isolated `pnpm test:auth-http:local` harness, using cached PostgreSQL 18 executables and the pinned disposable Redis container, passes 200 integration tests (17 Database, 164 API and 19 Worker). Seven new Chat cases cover duplicate/concurrent writers, reconstruction, stable keyset pagination, member/role/terminated denial, atomic outbox rollback, legacy visibility and grants, shared quotas/Redis unavailability, post-commit two-node fanout, missing-ack reconnect recovery, revoked sockets and content-free BullMQ delivery. A new Worker integration case covers shared decoder/claim/publication and queue storage. Upgrade/redeploy/schema-drift checks preserve populated old-release rows; a real database restart preserves exact messages/streams alongside prior durable records. Chromium passes 14 ordinary scenarios and two separate preview-isolation scenarios. Chat cases cover plain-text XSS, identical ambiguous retries, game invalidation without Chat reload, reconnect pagination, Bot read-only status and lifecycle/storage clearing. Axe reports zero violations on tested chat; the 320px Chat screenshot has no horizontal overflow and was inspected visually.
- **Corrections / limits / mandatory gate:** verification caught and corrected the new foreign key's missing Prisma-compatible `ON UPDATE CASCADE`, a test's incorrect assumption that missing acknowledgement keeps its socket connected, Profile navigation for logout, the Socket emitter's TypeScript event-wait helper, and scoped formatting. No earlier-phase prerequisite was repaired. Chat remains opt-in; production catalog/Socket/gameplay activation and 7C are unchanged. Final OD-005 retention/report/mute/age, storage-growth policy, staged HTTPS/Telegram WebView E2E and load/soak remain open. Disposable services are cleaned by their harnesses. No production migration, commit, push, deployment or live Telegram change. Phase 15B remains Pending; stop at the mandatory phase gate.

### Phase 15B — Match results, user statistics, and leaderboard

- **Objective:** complete the `Finish -> Stats` portion of the MVP loop from durable match results.
- **Scope:** standard result persistence, idempotent stats projection, per-game aggregates, all-time/period dimensions, profile stats and leaderboard REST/UI.
- **Expected deliverables:** Leaderboard/statistics module, Worker consumer, APIs, UI, rebuild procedure, and tests.
- **Dependencies:** Phase 15A and Phase 11B.
- **Acceptance criteria:** PostgreSQL results remain source of truth; duplicate finish events do not double-count; projections can be rebuilt; no Redis leaderboard is added without measured need.
- **Required tests and validations:** result/projection idempotency tests, ranking/tie/pagination tests, rebuild integration test, REST/UI tests, lint/typecheck/build.
- **Status:** Completed — owner-approved baseline implemented and locally verified on 2026-10-02; stopped at the mandatory phase gate.
- **Authorization / prerequisite assessment (2026-10-02):** owner explicitly authorized Phase 15B. Started on clean `main` at `c858bbe` (Phase15A). Read the four required sources in full, including the named reviewed architecture in `/home/mehdi/Downloads`; the canonical repository architecture retains the previously approved amendment. Inspected the actual manifests/task graph, completed Chat dependency, standard Coup result mapper, atomic Match finish writer, strict versioned finish input, outbox queues, consumer receipt transaction, database ownership/grants and Leaderboard scaffolds. No earlier-phase prerequisite defect was found or repaired. ADR-024 explicitly defers result-table projection and leaderboard behavior to this phase; those unimplemented deliverables are not incorrectly reported as a Phase 11B defect. Phase 7C remains the existing live-activation gate.
- **Historical in-phase decision gate (resolved by owner approval):** architecture section 17 permits PostgreSQL Coup Wins and requires game/metric/period dimensions, but does not choose account credit after Bot takeover, the period calendar, ranking ties or leaderboard identity visibility. ADR-011 explicitly leaves period calendars open. `MATCH_FINISHED_V1` contains player IDs, placements, winner, metrics, finish time and version pins, without human/Bot control metadata. Match membership separately downgrades a replaced human to spectator; that read permission does not establish a statistics-credit rule. At assessment time both Leaderboard public surfaces were unimplemented, and no accepted statistics ADR existed. These choices affect visible results, durable aggregates and public contracts, so the execution protocol's Handling Ambiguity section requires an owner decision before implementation. This is a Phase 15B policy gate, not an earlier-phase blocker.
- **Proposed temporary baseline — accepted by owner approval:** count one completed game for every roster account in a valid engine-driven FINISHED result and one win for its recorded winner. Credit stays with the original account after Bot takeover, including any subsequent Bot win; TERMINATED Matches earn no plays or wins. Derive losses as completed games minus wins for Coup. Preserve standard placements/reason/metrics and version pins as immutable Match-owned result truth; statistics remain a rebuildable asynchronous projection, never an authority for Match outcome.
- **Proposed periods / ranks — accepted by owner approval:** serve all-time and UTC weeks starting Monday at 00:00, assigning a result by its server-recorded `finishedAt`, not queue arrival or processing time. Retain the period dimension without inventing a seasonal calendar. Rank by wins descending, give equal wins the same competition rank (1, 1, 3), and use opaque user ID ascending solely for deterministic ordering within a tie. Include accounts with completed games even when their win count is zero. Return bounded pages of at most 50 entries; pagination reflects current aggregates and can change when results arrive, so the UI offers refresh rather than promising a frozen ranking snapshot.
- **Proposed read / privacy baseline — accepted by owner approval:** require the existing valid session and active account for both REST endpoints. `/v1/me/stats` returns only the authenticated user's per-game completed games, wins and derived losses. `/v1/leaderboards/:game` returns rank, wins, opaque account ID and the existing account display name; no Telegram identifier, private settings, Match state, hidden information or chat content is exposed. Display-name lookup uses a reviewed public Users port, never a foreign table query. No rating, skill score, economy, seasonal activation, new account-erasure/retention policy or live-game activation is selected.
- **Approved implementation approach:** record an accepted ADR before freezing these contracts. Matches writes standard result records in the existing atomic finish transaction and owns a bounded public result-read/recovery port. Leaderboard owns aggregate/projection metadata and idempotent receipt effects; Worker connects the owning public ports to the real finish queue. A bounded, fenced rebuild obtains immutable results through the Matches port and publishes a complete projection without exposing partially rebuilt counts. Reuse PostgreSQL, current queue/session/rate-limit conventions and additive migrations; no Redis leaderboard or new dependency is needed. Preserve queued v1 finish inputs. Recovery of existing valid Phase 11B finish envelopes can materialize their results idempotently; older Matches without a validated durable finish input require a separately reviewed backfill rather than fabricated outcomes or silent engine replay.
- **Historical assessment validation:** the API test command with an attempted name filter forwarded a literal `--` to Vitest, so the actual result is the full existing API suite: 745/745 tests across 33 files, including finish-input privacy/validation and pinned result adapter coverage. `pnpm --filter @hambazi/database test` passes 22/22 unit tests; `pnpm --filter @hambazi/worker test` passes 29/29 unit tests. `pnpm format:check` and `git diff --check` pass; diff/status review confirms this plan is the sole changed file. The existing PostgreSQL finish rollback/replay and concurrent consumer receipt/effect tests were inspected, not rerun. These checks confirm prerequisites; they are not evidence of an implemented statistics consumer, ranking, migration, rebuild or browser flow.
- **Historical assessment-only stop — superseded by approval:** only the plan changed at the policy gate; implementation was deferred pending the baseline decision. The owner subsequently approved that proposal, authorizing the work below. Phase 16 remains Pending and unauthorized.

- **Owner approval / delivered implementation (2026-10-02):** owner approved the proposed Phase 15B baseline. [ADR-030](adr/030-temporary-statistics-baseline.md) records it, including the narrow public Users eligibility/name edge and additive generation storage. Matches now commits immutable standard finish/result rows with its existing atomic writer and unchanged v1 outbox input; the public result port corroborates old queued envelopes against durable Match/outbox/roster truth. Leaderboard owns idempotent receipt/application/count effects. Worker has a real opt-in BullMQ finish consumer, bounded drain and trusted rebuild/abandoned-build recovery commands. Rebuilds recover retained inputs, scan bounded immutable pages, dual-project overlapping live deliveries and atomically switch a fenced generation. Existing user_game_stats keys/callbacks and old writer/job shapes remain intact for release overlap.

### Phase 16A — Shared telemetry safety and critical-path correlation

- **Required tests and validations:** projection/redaction/adversarial tests, concurrent HTTP/Socket context tests, durable outbox/real BullMQ propagation and retry/privacy tests, compatibility checks, frozen install, lint/boundaries/typecheck/build and diff review.

Raw objects/errors/content, unknown fields/events and invalid identifiers never enter the logger; observer failure is best effort. ### Phase 16B — Dependency health and safe service draining

- **Objective:** make service readiness and shutdown reflect dependency/admission state.
- **Scope / deliverables:** liveness/readiness module and validated operational configuration, API/Bot/Worker dependency probes, fail/degrade behavior, idempotent admission stop and bounded HTTP/Socket/job drains, lifecycle documentation.
- **Dependencies:** 16A; health exposure/access and dependency readiness must preserve existing Redis-outage session/logout behavior and opt-in capability gates. Any material public/operational policy selection follows the protocol's decision gate.
- **Acceptance / required validation:** liveness independent of downstream outages; readiness false before new admission stops; accepted work drains before resource closure; dependency failure/recovery and actual in-flight shutdown integration tests, lint/typecheck/build.
- **Status:** Completed (implementation and available local verification)
- **Authorization / assessment (2026-10-02):** owner explicitly approved continuing with 16B. Started from clean `main` at `0c34239` (Phase 16A), ahead of the remote by one commit. Rechecked the recorded 16A completion, current service composition/lifecycle, durable infrastructure ports, queue adapters and approved Redis-outage session/logout behavior. No earlier-phase prerequisite repair, live activation or 16C work is authorized or required. ADR-032 documents reversible private health/configuration and drain details; final operational/deployment decisions remain open.
- **Delivered:** shared monotonic service health, bounded/coalesced read-only probes and optional exact-GET loopback listeners; an API Health public application surface; role-specific validated port/probe/drain configuration. PostgreSQL uses a fixed infrastructure SELECT 1 transaction, Redis limiters use abortable PING, and pinned BullMQ 6 probes use its public backend ready state and an INFO round trip with all details discarded. API reports DEGRADED while its durable dependency remains usable during enabled Redis limiter loss; Worker requires every enabled queue/publisher and PostgreSQL; Bot uses an authenticated private TLS readiness probe without spending onboarding admission counters. Local health never mounts on product ingress and ports have no default.
- **Drain behavior / compatibility:** readiness becomes NOT_READY before synchronous admission stop. HTTP tracks application handler settlement independently of aborted response sockets; already admitted Socket commands finish while new intents/handshakes are denied. Queue/recovery/outbox work stops locally, accepted writes/jobs drain, and owning clients/database close after settlement. Feature-specific automation/statistics deadline failures do not cause premature database teardown. A fetch racing pause returns to waiting without spending a failure attempt, using the pinned library's public transition; shared schedules, stable job IDs and v1 payloads remain intact. Root close calls are idempotent and bounded for callers; deadline failure does not cancel pending application work or claim successful drain. Background teardown may complete later, with a supervised hard failure exit one second after the configured drain budget. Existing 15-second API/Worker and 30-second Bot budgets remain compatibility defaults, not final SLOs.
- **Contracts / documentation:** ADR-032 and `docs/architecture/health-and-draining.md` record ownership, local-access constraints, dependency/degrade semantics, compatible settings, shutdown ordering and limits. Updated current Auth/Bot/observability documentation. Added a schemaVersion=1 operational OpenAPI document/baseline and an additive service-authenticated private Bot health path; the existing Bot contract generator/check now verifies both. Existing product baselines and all prior paths/schemas remain unchanged; the root API test inputs include the new baseline. No dependency version, lockfile resolution, database migration/grant, job schema or deployment change.
- **Validation:** `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, scoped tests/typechecks/builds, root lint/format/architecture audit (14 workspaces / 199 product source files), root `pnpm typecheck` (14 scripts / 21 tasks including prerequisites), `pnpm build` (12 tasks), all seven existing contract commands including the new health check, and diff review pass. `pnpm exec turbo run test --concurrency=1 -- --testTimeout=15000` passes 1,535 workspace tests; `pnpm test:infra` passes seven more (1,542 total). Bot TLS integration passes 38 tests, including backend readiness loss/recovery. The documented isolated PostgreSQL/Redis harness passes 216 integration tests (17 Database, 173 API, 26 Worker), including real API health listener isolation, Redis restart with durable session access, queue connection loss/recovery, accepted PostgreSQL work during a drain deadline and a pause-vs-fetch race with only one failure attempt followed by successful recovery. Added 18 workspace tests and three integration cases; existing lifecycle/compatibility/privacy regressions remain green. Harness services were cleaned up.
- **Corrections / remaining limits / mandatory gate:** intermediate compilation corrected the exact-path union check, an inserted backend method parameter, a disabled-listener fixture and assumptions about older BullMQ client/PING APIs; the final adapter follows the installed v6 public contract. Corrected one formatting issue and a lifecycle test fixture, and review replaced failure-attempt consumption on a pause race with tested requeue behavior. No earlier product implementation was expanded. The inherited Coup model timeout still requires the explicit 15-second runner override; default `pnpm check` is not claimed green. The owner subsequently approved scoped local remediation using supported minimal variants/base strategy with locale, data and role compatibility checks. This approval was not vulnerability-risk acceptance, production deployment or authorization for 17A2. Sanitized historical evidence is retained under `previousReview` in `docs/security/local-image-findings.json`.
- **Approved remediation / usable outcome:** ADR-035 pins official Redis 8.10.2/alpine3.23 and a local PostgreSQL 18.6/alpine3.24 derivative. Locked signed Alpine `su-exec` replaces the vulnerable Go helper; the HTTP/2 library is patched. A minimal private network-disabled build verifies input hashes and reproduces the locked manifest digest on the documented Linux amd64 builder. Both final Compose images now report **zero findings in every severity**, without exceptions, VEX or ignore-unfixed. The scanner verifies the local lock/Compose/Dockerfile agreement and reports safe lower-severity IDs too. No live volume or root `.env` was modified.
- **Data/locale boundary:** new clusters use UTF8/builtin `C.UTF-8`; populated legacy directories are refused before startup with exit 78. The existing default named volume is preserved, and optional `POSTGRES_DATA_VOLUME` selects a separate fresh target for an authorized logical transfer. An initialization check writes the private marker only after actual locale/role bootstrap; it must never be forged. The transition runbook preserves the old source and requires reconciliation, custom locale/extension checks and rollback planning. No real-data migration, physical-volume upgrade, production locale or backup policy was selected.

Private disposable Compose config validates default/project/fresh volume names. ### Phase 17A2 — Distributed admission and transport authorization

- **Objective / scope:** harden pre-authentication HTTP/Engine.IO and authenticated Socket admission, bounded work/abuse handling and the authorization matrix while preserving approved outage behavior, durable logout and module-owned permissions.
- **Expected deliverables / dependencies:** 17A1; explicit validated rate policy, Redis adapters, HTTP/Socket abuse/identity/membership/state tests and compatible documentation. Production quotas and material outage changes require owner review before public behavior is frozen.
- **Acceptance / required validation:** atomic shared quotas, pre-expensive-work rejection, malformed/unauthorized intents cannot mutate, no hidden output; real Redis/multi-node tests, transport schema/contract compatibility, information-flow, lint/typecheck/build.
- **Status:** Completed
- **Authorization / prerequisite review (2026-10-02):** owner explicitly authorized the next phase after the completed 17A1 gate. Required documents and Git candidate were re-read; 17A1 changes are preserved. Only existing auth/outage, transport, module-owned permissions, Redis ACL and isolated integration prerequisites were inspected. No earlier phase was reopened. No production values or approved outage policies were changed. 17A3/17B and the 7C activation gate remain unauthorized.
- **Implemented:** explicit validated transport profile with four Redis per-minute quotas and six local work/connection/subscription limits; complete profile required for production API startup, no defaults. Realtime owns the public atomic Redis IP/user adapter with canonical IPv4/IPv6/UUID subjects, hashed keys, capped non-renewing 60-second windows, bounded offline/command work and safe errors. API composition wires its public IP port, health probe and drain/close lifecycle without enabling gameplay. HTTP admission precedes parsing/JWT/DB work; aborted clients retain actual handler capacity. Existing durable inspection/logout and owner-specific GET policies continue under local bounds when admission is unavailable; mutations fail closed.
- **Transport / privacy:** Engine.IO middleware covers polling/upgrades/preflight and empty/invalid sid attempts independently of Fastify, with trusted IP/CIDR forwarding and production HTTPS checks. Namespace authentication and duplicate attempts are bounded; reservations remain through the deferred connection callback. Socket IP/user checks precede expensive work; unknown/malformed/unacknowledged messages count without mutation. Queues, subscription cardinality, coalesced heartbeat and one reserved disconnect cleanup are bounded; retiring cleanup retains connection capacity against churn. Owner permissions/state/turn/idempotency remain authoritative. Recheck durable bound identity/expiry after private reads and Chat projection; membership loss removes Match cursor and closes Presence-enabled transports to release retained leases. Accepted outcomes and durable retry/resync/drain remain intact.
- **Files / decisions:** new Config schema/parser/tests, Realtime Redis/peer adapter/tests, HTTP admission adapter and synthetic admission fixture; modified API composition/Gateway/public exports, transport/HTTP/real-session/process fixtures and isolated suite list. Added ADR-036 and security policy/authorization matrix; updated architecture, threat model, risk register and documentation-only environment comments. No dependency, public wire baseline, database migration, game rule, takeover or retention change. SR-003/004 are closed for local implementation; production policy and capacity evidence remain gated.
- **Verification:** focused API/Config tests and final workspace pipeline `pnpm exec turbo run test --concurrency=1 -- --testTimeout=15000` pass **1,596 tests**, including **817 API**, **241 Config** and existing Coup/Observability information-flow/replay suites; unchanged tasks may reuse valid Turbo cache. Added **45 workspace cases** and **3 real Redis integration cases**. `SECURITY_SCANNER_DIR=/tmp/hambazi-17a-tools pnpm test:infra` passes **21 cases**, including real scanner canaries. Final `TEST_POSTGRES_MODE=compose TEST_RESTORE_DRILL=isolated pnpm test:auth-http:local` with the existing binary/share/library environment passes **221 integration cases (17 Database, 178 API, 26 Worker)** and the **29-table** restore drill against the pinned remediated local images. Includes shared atomic quotas across two clients, two HTTP applications and two actual Socket servers, bounded malformed counters/TTL, forged JWT admission before session work, quota outages/restart, durable logout, owner race/idempotency and recipient/takeover behavior. Root lint/format, architecture audit (14 workspaces/210 product sources), root typechecks (21 tasks), final API source/test typecheck, root build (12 tasks), seven API contract commands, dependency audit and final candidate secret scan pass. No contract or Prisma schema diff.
- **Corrections / limits:** fixed new middleware types, partial synthetic fixture policy, incorrect issue status/duplicate polling path, readonly projection fixture and a drain test that incorrectly required a connect-error packet after transport teardown. Review also closed empty-sid/preflight admission bypass, canonical mapped-CIDR compatibility, namespace reservation and cleanup-churn gaps within this phase's hardening scope. Final counts were explicitly reconciled. No new dependency/install or migration was needed; integration applies existing migrations only to disposable fixtures. The inherited default five-second Coup model timeout remains; the explicit 15-second runner is used, and default `pnpm check` is not claimed green. No UI change or controlled public HTTPS/WebView staging exists, so browser/staging E2E was not rerun. Raw network/TLS floods, numeric production quotas, ingress/provider, reconnect-storm/load/soak and external operational evidence remain owner/later-phase gates. Full Compose host connectivity remains SR-013; no earlier networking work was repaired.
- **Mandatory gate:** 17A2 is **Completed** locally; parent 17A stays **In Progress**, 17A3/17B stay **Pending**. Existing uncommitted 17A1 work is preserved. No commit, push, deployment, real-data/production migration or live Telegram action occurred. Stop and request explicit authorization for 17A3 only.

- **Authorization / prerequisite review (2026-10-03):** owner explicitly approved proceeding after the completed 17A2 gate. Read the four required documents in full, including the original reviewed architecture in `/home/mehdi/Downloads`, and compared the maintained architecture's approved ADR-019/030 amendments. Inspected Git on `main` at `bac9c3c`, actual manifests/workspace/task graph, the completed transport boundary, Web bootstrap/runtime/browser fixtures and existing admin/activation gates. All uncommitted 17A1/17A2 work is preserved; no earlier phase was reimplemented.
- **Historical blocking prerequisite evidence (2026-10-03):** `apps/web/index.html` loads the official `https://telegram.org/js/telegram-web-app.js` before HamBazi bootstrap. The reviewed SDK reads/writes `__telegram__initParams` through `window.sessionStorage` (download SHA-256 `3549138a7934039fe7dfd1291a4ee739bd2b705a614308053a8b08a87d85c451`). Its launch cache includes `tgWebAppData`, the raw initialization credential, and restores it when a subsequent launch URL omits that field. `apps/web/src/platform/telegram.ts` removes launch parameters from URL/history but does not prevent or remove this cache. The existing browser `launch` helper stubs the SDK response with empty JavaScript, so its empty-storage assertions do not exercise the real bridge. This contradicts architecture sections 14.6/16.2, AGENTS section 9, and the completed 14A privacy claim; it is an earlier bootstrap prerequisite defect, not an absent hosting decision.
- **Reproduction / safe result:** downloaded the public official SDK over verified HTTPS and served the existing ordinary Web app through its declared loopback `dev` script on port 43173. A disposable Chromium context loaded the actual SDK with synthetic initialization data, without signing in or contacting any product API. After HamBazi bootstrap, `launchRemovedFromUrl=true` and `rawInitDataPersisted=true`; after reload, `cleanUrl=true`, `rawInitDataRestoredFromStorage=true` and `rawInitDataStillPersisted=true`. Output contains booleans only, never credentials. Browser context and task-owned Vite processes were closed. No product source, dependency, contract, migration, admin route, deployment or live Telegram setting changed.
- **Historical required owner scope / stop (before correction approval):** the starter and execution protocol require stopping when a completed prerequisite is genuinely incorrect and blocks this phase. Request a narrowly scoped prerequisite correction of the Web/official-SDK bootstrap boundary, plus real-SDK storage/reload/logout tests, before resuming 17A3. Prevent raw initData persistence from the first SDK execution, remove any legacy credential cache, preserve memory-only sessions and supported Telegram launch behavior, and retain all existing preview/activation boundaries. A post-bootstrap deletion alone cannot prove the credential was never persisted. No corrective mechanism or new ADR has been implemented or silently approved; final hosting, admin provider, retention and rights decisions remain open. Phase 17B remains Pending.

- **Prerequisite resolved (2026-10-03):** the owner explicitly approved the focused 17A3P correction below. Guarded integrity-pinned loading now prevents SDK credential storage before execution, deletes accessible legacy copies and passes actual-SDK development/production tests. ADR-037 records the decision and limits. This resolved the reported local blocker at the 17A3P gate. The explicit resumption authorization above then permitted the remaining browser policy and administrative design, delivered below.

- **Delivered on owner resumption (2026-10-03):** added a pure Web-owned browser-policy generator and Vite build/preview plugin. Ordinary builds emit a versioned public `security-headers.json`; boundary/preview checks validate bounded inputs, regenerate exact headers against the central SDK pin and reject missing/malformed/inconsistent evidence with fixed errors. Production accepts canonical HTTPS origins only and derives explicit WSS destinations; default inputs choose no deployment domain and deny framing. The explicit loopback-test profile requires local Web/API origins and refuses public preview binding. Enforced CSP denies inline/eval scripts, event attributes, unspecified network/resources, objects, frames, workers, bases and forms; permits self scripts plus the exact integrity-pinned SDK hash, exact configured connect/frame ancestors, and SDK-compatible inline CSS. Added no-referrer, nosniff, disabled unused permissions, no-store and production host-only HSTS without conflicting Mini App X-Frame-Options. API's existing known-origin CORS/frame denial remains intact.
- **Build/privacy decisions:** ADR-038 records restrictive policy, exact external SDK hash/SRI and the deliberate inline-CSS allowance for real Telegram iframe styles/themes. Browser-only Zod interpreted configuration runs before schema imports, avoiding its eval probe without changing backend/wire validation. Web Turbo builds now hash/pass the four public policy environment inputs and hash Web-local `.env*`, so policy changes cannot reuse stale cached output. Artifact consistency is not cryptographic provenance or authorization of origin choices; approved inventory and immutable release/actual edge enforcement remain 18A/18B. No CSP raw reports, telemetry payload capture, new dependency, session strategy or production origin is introduced.
- **Administrative design:** ADR-039 and `docs/security/admin-security-design.md` provide separate identity/audience/keys, verified MFA and step-up, private ingress/trusted peers, deny-by-default explicit capabilities, owner-port authority and append-only content-free audit. Durable admission precedes a mutation; owning atomic effect/receipt/outbox and deduplicated outcome records recover retries/crashes without cross-module SQL or network I/O in transactions. Provider, operator/role assignments, time bounds, approval separation and retention remain owner decisions. No operator implementation, route, grant or mutation is enabled; 7C must implement/verify this design before activation.
- **Validation on resumption:** frozen-lockfile installation passes without manifest/lockfile changes. Web source/test typechecks and **62 unit cases in eight files** pass (20 new policy cases). Full API tests pass **818 cases in 38 files**, including one new case checking 12 unauthenticated/valid-player/service-shaped requests to absent operator mutations; API source/test typecheck and all **eight** current contract-document checks pass. Observability passes **32** safe telemetry tests. Scoped Coup compatibility/private-view/reconnect verification passes **7**, with 72 unrelated cases intentionally skipped. The actual built-app browser configuration passes **33** cases (16 platform, 10 real-SDK privacy/control, seven new CSP/header/framing/resource cases), including successful sign-in, polling and WebSocket upgrade, dynamic Telegram parent CSS/theme and inline/eval/network/frame denials. Synthetic preview passes **two** cases. A final parser/plugin change was followed by passing 62 Web units/typecheck and two focused built-header/transport browser cases.
- **Additional final verification:** root lint, architecture audit (**14 workspaces / 214 product source files**), format, diff review and root build (**12 successful tasks**, unchanged prerequisite tasks may reuse cache) pass. Final ordinary production output has no loopback fixture destination/profile and selects no final deployment origins. Five public-input dry runs verify all four configured environment values alter the Web Turbo hash and Web `.env.example` is an input. Nine negative build-boundary/preview executions deny missing, malformed, oversized, inconsistent artifacts and public loopback-test binding, with no synthetic artifact canary in diagnostics; original ignored output is restored. Re-provisioned official Gitleaks 8.30.1 passes the pinned archive/binary hashes; reachable history and **581** current candidate files report zero secret findings through the safe CLI. The interruption removed private temporary tools/SDK/checkpoint files; the scanner was re-provisioned for final verification, without rewriting prior product work or claiming a surviving hash snapshot.

Corrected header application, configured interpreted validation and adjusted only the synthetic harness (HTTPS framing uses routed actual built responses, not real TLS evidence). Corrected the new HTTP expectation to the existing NOT_FOUND code and documented the expected worker denial in its empty catch for lint. Final relevant checks pass. No controlled HTTPS/Telegram account/mobile WebView/edge inventory was provided; provider headers/cache/log redaction and real MFA/RBAC/audit/grant drills remain external/7C gates. No DB/Redis integration, container scan/build/restore or migration was repeated: no backend product code, dependency, image, persistence or worker behavior changed in 17A3; retain dated 17A1/17A2 evidence rather than claiming fresh container results. ### Phase 17A3P — Telegram bootstrap privacy prerequisite correction

- **Required tests and validations:** actual hash-verified SDK browser tests including native storage interception, legacy/reload/logout/error cases, Web unit/typecheck/build and ordinary/preview browser regressions, lint/architecture/format and diff review; controlled Telegram/WebView staging remains conditional on its unavailable environment.

Fix the Telegram Mini App bootstrap/privacy prerequisite first.” Complete only this corrective prerequisite and stop before resuming 17A3. Previously reviewed full architecture/plan and current AGENTS/protocol, Git candidate, manifests/task graph and relevant Web source were checked; all prior work is preserved. No further earlier-phase repair is authorized.

- **Delivered (2026-10-03):** removed the parser-loaded SDK tag and added Web-owned storage protection followed by asynchronous official-SDK loading. Original storage methods delete and verify accessible legacy caches; immutable document-lifetime get/set guards block only `__telegram__initParams`, leaving theme/other keys alone. The loader uses a single reviewed SHA-384 pin, anonymous CORS, no referrer and a ten-second deadline. Cleanup/protection/SDK/integrity failure supplies no credential; explicit bootstrap input prevents fallback to a rejected or late global bridge. Existing URL/history cleanup, memory-only runtime, invitation, theme/viewport/insets and ready/expand remain intact. HMR disposal fences delayed initialization. Local preview injection now works with this loader and remains synthetic/dev-only/loopback-bound; production checks reject parser-loaded SDKs and require guard/pin presence alongside preview exclusion.
- **Decisions / review evidence:** ADR-037 records the narrow reserved-key hook, fail-closed loading, original SDK SHA-256 and pin-maintenance procedure. The verified public SDK response supports anonymous CORS. Unmodified hash-verified bytes are provisioned outside the repository for browser tests; no SDK fork, vendoring or product dependency was added. A bare-SDK negative control proves the credential/native-write probes detect the original defect. Protected tests use original-method reads to prove physical deletion, not just masked reads. Updated the Mini App security documentation, threat model and risk register (SR-014); broader SR-005/006/011 work stays with 17A3.
- **Validation:** `pnpm --filter @hambazi/web typecheck` and `test` pass (42 unit tests in seven files, including 18 new storage/loader/bootstrap cases). The production browser configuration builds with a synthetic API destination; the final ordinary build restores the configured normal output.

SDK clean-launch BackButton and runner color warnings are expected and contain no credential payload. The SDK may retain its initData getter in document memory until closure; HamBazi logout/disposal clears its own references, and clean reload cannot restore it. - **Browser fixture / ownership:** extended the existing disposable harness with `TEST_RELEASE_BROWSER=isolated`, a separately migrated browser database in the owned cluster, compiled API/Worker fixtures and actual built Mini App under explicit loopback policy. Four MVP Playwright cases drive verified synthetic Telegram onboarding, Invite and Find admission, explicit ready, real server Coup commands/finish, chat escaping, Worker reconstruction, immutable result/statistics reconciliation, offline catch-up, forged launch rejection, lost-create-response stable retry and API drain/restart. A fifth case verifies intentionally failing scalar assertions/private-button actions exclude a private canary and ARIA snapshots from captured diagnostics and generated reports. Stored production catalog remains disabled. Public ports own all game transitions; audits expose only IDs/status/versions/counts. Receipt counts use separate existing Worker grants; child Worker receives only its credentials. API/Worker telemetry discards payloads, browser trace/video/screenshots are off, and final game-page helpers prevent accessibility/click-error capture of private cards. See [release suites](testing/release-suites.md) for commands and the evidence matrix.
- **Corrections / scope:** intermediate failures were in new fixture inputs, selectors and assertions: an auth limit exceeded its constructor bounds, an overly broad startup stage pointed at the database, a fault test used the wrong options variable, readiness raced another recipient's update, a chat button label was incorrect, queue order differed from launch order, Chromium offline emulation kept an established WebSocket, disconnect cleared the renderable snapshot, and definitive drain denial did not retain an ambiguous retry. Corrected the fixture/runner, waited for current recipient/UI state, used real API transport closure and existing Worker-only receipt grants. Root contract generation needed repository formatting afterward and leaves reviewed/generated artifacts unchanged. Direct Node SDK retrieval timed out; proxy-aware retrieval supplied the unmodified integrity-verified official bytes. No preceding product defect was reimplemented or security grant broadened. Staging HTTPS, Telegram mobile WebViews, deployment origin/headers, live Bot delivery, final policies and Phase 7C remain external gates; 17C capacity/soak and 18A CI work remain unauthorized.

Socket.IO acknowledgement parsing now includes the actual `/v1` namespace, and accepted moves wait for engine revision rather than presence-only Match version changes. Definitive stale-version denials synchronize before bounded new intents. ### Phase 17BP — Private-room-to-Match prerequisite

- **Objective / scope:** connect an authorized frozen private roster to one durable pinned Match, explicit player readiness/startup and terminal room closure, with idempotent retry and bounded trusted recovery.
- **Expected deliverables:** Matches-owned creation/orchestration port and repository, trusted Rooms start/recovery projections, optional existing Socket delegation, additive migration, ADR/architecture documentation and focused unit/transaction/race/recovery/transport tests.
- **Dependencies:** existing Rooms foundation/invites, Match writer/pinned adapters and realtime public ports; no earlier completed phase is reimplemented or production catalog activated.
- **Acceptance criteria:** unauthorized/stale starts cannot create a Match; competing/ambiguous starts preserve one complete roster and original pins; failures between owner commits can be recovered without a connected player; explicit readiness initializes exactly once; terminal Match truth closes only its bound room; prior wire contracts and old inserts remain compatible.
- **Required tests and validations:** focused unit tests and real isolated PostgreSQL/Socket integration, rollback/unknown-commit/reconstruction/race tests, migration upgrade/redeploy/drift/grants/restart, frozen install, scoped typecheck/build, lint/boundaries/format and unchanged contract checks; unavailable staged Telegram E2E remains explicit.
- **Status:** Completed (implementation/local verification; production activation remains gated)
- **Authorization / implementation approach (2026-10-03):** owner approved only this prerequisite after the Phase 17B blocker. Required full-document reviews from the same conversation remain applicable; re-read AGENTS/protocol, inspected the current plan/unchanged architecture and actual manifests/workspace/task graph, and preserved the sole existing plan modification on `main` at `fb35f91`. ADR-040 records separate owner commits, the existing unique room Match identity, original frozen revision/pins, explicit ready-before-activation and trusted recovery. No new operator authority, readiness timeout, timer/ruleset value or production activation is selected.
- **Delivered (2026-10-03):** Matches now owns an opt-in private creation/orchestration port. Rooms exposes a validated frozen roster and bounded STARTING/STARTED recovery pages only through trusted public ports. Host authorization and original receipt stay with Rooms; Matches atomically creates one PRIVATE_ROOM record, all ordered seats and the existing state-free wakeup with trusted version pins. The additive thirteenth migration pins the original room start revision; old inserts remain compatible without backfill or extra grants. New starts reserve confirmation/closure revisions. The existing Match writer commits WAIT before trusted room confirmation, explicit human readiness before START/ACTIVATE, and unchanged game/result processing before bound-room closure. Optional Socket delegation exposes the existing usable flow and startedMatchId to the current Mini App without changing public schemas or default runtime/catalog wiring.
- **Recovery / compatibility:** retries preserve original owner acknowledgements and stable intent identities. Reconstructed trusted resume/recovery repairs freezes before creation, unknown creation commits, WAIT/confirmation/startup gaps and terminal closure, preserving original roster/pins during catalog maintenance. Bounded keyset pages continue after per-room fixed-code errors; callers restart at the first page after a completed pass. Missing adapters, legacy provenance and conflicting bindings fail closed. No public recovery route, automatic scheduler interval, readiness deadline, fresh game policy or operator mutation is inferred. ADR-040 and the private-room Matches architecture guide document ownership, live composition requirements and compatible rollback.
- **Local validation:** frozen-lockfile install, Prisma format/client generation/database build, scoped API source/test typechecks and unit tests, database unit tests, root lint/format/architecture/typecheck/build and API contract checks pass. API unit coverage is **833 tests / 39 files**, including **15** added cases (12 private orchestration, two Rooms projections/pages and one revision guard); database unit coverage is **24**. The architecture audit covers **14 workspaces / 217 product source files**, root typecheck completes **21 tasks**, and root build completes **12 tasks** with valid cache reuse. All **eight generated contract documents** remain compatible without baseline changes. `SECURITY_SCANNER_DIR=/tmp/hambazi-17a3-tools pnpm test:infra` passes **21/21**, including the pinned local secret-scanner canary; this is not a fresh dependency/history/container scan.
- **Isolated integration evidence:** the declared `pnpm test:auth-http:local` harness with the existing extracted PostgreSQL 18 binary/share/library environment and disposable pinned Redis passes **234 tests** (**17 database / 191 API / 26 Worker**), including **13** new private-room cases. They verify real invite admission/host guards, competing starts, rollback at each creation write, actual unknown-commit injection, reconstructed recovery/original pins, concurrent final readiness, real Coup finish/result/outbox, room-close repair, bounded recovery pages, legacy inserts and real Socket routing/recipient-safe resync. Every injected failure is asserted to have occurred. All **13** migrations deploy/redeploy without Prisma-visible drift; populated previous-schema expansion, API/Worker least-privilege grants and complete durable Room/Match/result/statistics restart comparisons pass. No application environment file, public game activation, real Telegram credential or live database is used.
- **Corrections / limits / mandatory gate:** early new-fixture failures came from a fault wrapper routing non-Matches owners through the Matches scope, concurrent fixture setup and an assumption that every first attempt succeeds under bounded contention. Corrected owner forwarding, made setup sequential, and retried conflicted original intents while proving one logical Match/roster. Type inference/optional cursor handling and the new Socket listener were corrected without relaxing compiler or architecture rules. Final scoped checks and the full isolated harness pass. Full browser MVP/release suites belong to 17B and have not begun; staging HTTPS/Telegram WebView has no supplied environment. No broad root unit gate is claimed, and the inherited five-second full Coup model timeout remains. Before live activation, compose retained adapters and repeated bounded trusted recovery with approved scheduling/drain controls under Phase 7C. No dependency, public contract, engine rule, commit, push, deployment, live migration or Telegram change. **17BP is Completed locally; 17B is Pending at this mandatory resumption gate, and 17C remains Pending.**

### Phase 17CP — Focused Room admission investigation and repair

- **Objective:** identify and repair the cause of Room HTTP 503/non-retryable INTERNAL_ERROR in larger capacity setup without weakening admission guarantees.
- **Scope / authorization:** owner explicitly approved this prerequisite on 2026-10-05 and required root-cause evidence before quota/retry changes, targeted concurrency/load regression coverage and repeated 17C normal/stress validation from the existing checkpoint. Parent 17C remains Blocked throughout this work; staging stays deferred.

Total **48,000 offers / 29,205 accepted / 18,795 missed**, zero same-envelope retries, 822 reconciled Matches / 24,460 receipts; remaining accepted receipts are not reconciled, so no zero-loss claim. Stress reconnect succeeds **1,200/2,000**, with 800 failures. At final probe catchup slot age is **10,666.693 ms**, completed subscribe/catchup slot maxima **15,178.401 / 15,116.392 ms**, versus owner maxima **254.225 / 60.983 ms**. Pool has zero waiters / two idle connections, DB has zero blocked/lock-wait connections and maximum transaction age **12.123 ms**. All 355 generic Match counts equal callback domain conflicts; no Users/Auth/acquisition/Worker database failure occurs. Cleanup passes, only pre-existing laptop resources remain, release approval is false. No source/test/build/format work overlapped measurement.
- **Safe prerequisite gate:** source review establishes a 17C measurement gap: `move` starts its command/recipient clock before expired-turn recovery, catchup, terminal reconciliation and replacement admission, retaining that origin across retries. The reported command metric therefore can include time before the measured gameplay RPC is dispatched. The recipient metric also does not isolate commit-to-all-recipient delivery, as ADR-046 already records. The driver holds exactly the target pair count, marks a pair busy through replacement and skips that pair's offers; during turnover the completed normal/stress windows do not sustain the approved populations or command demand. These observations must remain distinct from a proven SQL defect or an isolated acknowledgement/dispatch SLO failure. The remaining catchup slot timeout is real and unresolved. Under the execution protocol's earlier-prerequisite/scope rule, stop before altering the 17C validation boundaries or workload construction. Proposed **17CT** below first corrects/attributes measurement while retaining targets, thresholds, retries, timing, security and workload history; its implementation is not authorized. **17CQ is Blocked, not Completed; 17C remains Blocked; staging is fully deferred.** All 655 checkpoint candidate files remain present; only focused changes and the new resync repository regression are added. No dependency/lockfile, migration/grant/index, engine, public contract, numerical policy, production configuration, deployment, publication, live Telegram operation or commit change is made.
- **Established cause / pre-repair evidence:** normal diagnostic `bbbeb791-d702-42b5-b383-e522a877b48e.json` stalls with both recipients connected and stale; all 23 generic Match counts correspond to callback `GAME_STATE_CONFLICT`. Stress diagnostic `b99cfc01-48dd-4235-8b79-6fc3c6c7001b.json` admits 2,000 clients/400 Matches and stalls during warmup, with one stale recipient and no disconnected recipient. Delivery service p95 upper bound is **200 ms**, while intent queue age p95 is **5,000 ms**, p99 **10,000 ms**, maximum **9,921 ms**, with zero publication failures. The hard-coded single delivery worker serializes all Matches. At driver stop, all 13 generic Match counts correspond to domain conflicts; **261 additional generic Match counts appear during teardown**, without corresponding callback rejections. The driver previously reported failure and closed sockets while offered moves/reads remained active. No Users error is reproduced in these two diagnostics; the two historical Users counts cannot be attributed from their original sanitized-only artifact, and are not evidence for a Users query repair or increased pool/timeouts.
- **Red-first concurrency regression / focused repair:** the declared isolated HTTP harness fails the new 16-table/32-wakeup real BullMQ/Socket test at the unrelated-table progress assertion while one recipient withholds an ACK. The consumer now accepts bounded explicit concurrency (1–100, compatible default 1); capacity Match delivery uses four, recorded in metadata. Per-socket serialization, ACK/cursor advancement, durable Match locks, session/membership/role checks, retry policy and admission bounds are unchanged. Test-owned tracking settles admitted moves and reads (including follow-up reads) before failure reporting/teardown and suppresses new invalidation reads after stop. No product database, engine, timer, pool, quota, wire contract or target change is made.

Full stress, reconnect storm, failure recovery and two-hour soak remain unproven. Cleanup passes.

### Phase 17CR — Room turnover admission prerequisite

- **Objective:** establish the cause of the newly observed Room admission failure during overlapping Match turnover, then repair only the proven cause.
- **Scope:** completed-Room accumulation and concurrent create/join/start/close/recovery interaction; privacy-safe per-operation conflict evidence and focused load/concurrency regression. Preserve security, Serializable isolation, idempotency and admission semantics. Do not infer a new retry, timeout, quota or capacity policy.
- **Expected deliverables:** reproducible cause, minimal owner-local repair, targeted regressions and unchanged normal/stress repeat evidence.
- **Dependencies:** owner authorization to reopen the earlier Room prerequisite; existing 17CP/CQ checkpoint and ADR-045/046 targets.
- **Acceptance criteria:** demonstrated cause before repair, existing security/correctness tests retained, focused concurrency regression passes, repeat outcomes reported without claiming 17C completion.
- **Required tests and validations:** Room transaction/admission unit and real PostgreSQL/Redis/Socket concurrency tests, relevant type/lint/boundary/contract/build checks, normal/stress profiles.
- **Status:** Completed (focused repair and targeted local validation; 17CQ/17C remain Blocked).
- **Owner authorization (2026-10-07):** reproduce overlapping Room admission, closure, start and recovery; identify the exact conflicting operations before changing retries, timeouts, pool sizes, quotas or isolation. Implement only the proven focused repair, add concurrency/load regressions, and repeat the approved 17C profiles from the existing checkpoint. All existing dirty work and deferred staging decisions are retained.

- **Established conflict / controlled reproduction:** the pre-repair join and an unrelated Room closure pause after their roster reads. Closure commits while the join advances its writes. The join aborts at `roomMember.deleteMany` and succeeds on its second existing attempt. The original metadata records tuple-level Room/roster predicate locks and page-level index locks; it does not alone prove a shared primary-key page. The explicit follow-up records **two relation-level `rooms` predicate locks**, with separate roster tuple locks: the point Room reads select table scans on the small relation, and the two status/revision updates establish cross-transaction dependencies before closure commits and the join rewrites members. The retained regression checks the shared coarse predicate and exact failing statement. The same interleaving with the roster rewrite removed instead aborts at the new membership insert (`40001`), so delta-only persistence is insufficient and was reverted. Only fixed statement/category/lock aggregates were inspected; no raw query, parameters, credential, error message or private game content was output. The exact failing statement and ordering are established in the controlled workflow; old aggregate artifacts alone cannot identify the particular SQL transaction that exhausted in a full run.
- **Focused repair / ADR:** [ADR-047](adr/047-room-transaction-ordering.md) records a Rooms-owned fixed `lockRooms()` primitive: `LOCK TABLE rooms IN SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE MODE` before the first snapshot-bearing statement of every Room repository transaction. It orders Room workflows across processes before SSI dependencies form, rather than increasing retries or weakening isolation. It is unavailable to other owners and fails outside a transaction; arbitrary SQL is not exposed. Room transactions can queue and occupy pool connections while waiting, a deliberate concurrency trade-off to validate at the unchanged targets. Existing five attempts, post-rollback jitter, time budgets, pool sizes, quotas, Serializable isolation, actor/invite/host/capacity/expiry/version checks, atomic membership/receipts, public non-retryable INTERNAL_ERROR and gameplay semantics remain. No schema/index/grant change is introduced.
- **Targeted regressions / final verification:** the original conflict is preserved behind a test-only lock bypass; the production regression holds a join and verifies two queued table-lock waiters before either reads, then checks that unrelated closure and a subsequent start see the committed roster without another transaction attempt. Eight waves overlap duplicate create/join/start, trusted confirmation/closure and bounded recovery over **64 Rooms**, yielding exactly **64 closed revision-four Rooms / 128 memberships / 192 receipts / 56 recovery projections**. The final declared `pnpm test:auth-http:local` harness with the existing native PostgreSQL environment passes **272 tests (17 database / 228 API / 27 Worker)**, including **22 Room tests**, all migration/redeployment/grant checks and durable restart/replay/idempotency coverage. Ten dependent builds succeed, seven cached. Database units **27/27**, API units **891/891**, database and API source/test typechecks, lint, the 14-workspace/217-source boundary audit, all eight unchanged generated contracts and formatting pass. Early diagnostic tests corrected an unsupported shared-index-page assertion using the observed relation-level predicate, test-only TypeScript casts and a database-unit key list for the new helper; the final assertions pass without weakening production behavior.
- **Combined normal/stress repeat:** unchanged `pnpm test:capacity:local --profile docs/testing/capacity-profile.mvp.json --normal-and-stress` produces `09276590-4126-4319-8e61-2231fd39404c.json` and exits one after **579.159 seconds**, first failing a connected **match:catchup** acknowledgement during 2x load. Normal warmup accepts **3,000/3,000** offers with no misses. The complete **300.082-second** normal window offers **15,000**, accepts **12,653** and misses **2,347** (busy), sustaining **42.165 accepted commands/sec**; minimum population is **996 clients / zero active Matches**, so normal population/throughput gates fail. REST/command/conservative intent-to-all-recipient p95 histogram upper bounds are **100/5,000/5,000 ms**; the last measure is not isolated committed-to-all-recipient latency. Normal reconnect succeeds **1,000/1,000**, but its p95 histogram upper bound is **60,000 ms**, with maximum **10,683.605 ms**: this does not pass the five-second gate and is not an exact 60-second p95 measurement. Total driver counts are **27,338 offers / 20,140 accepted / 7,198 missed**; only **537 Matches / 15,973 receipts** reconcile before interruption, so no complete zero-loss claim is made. Room records **two normalized transaction conflicts**, with no Room INTERNAL_ERROR or HTTP 503; at the first probe **26,594 completed Room transactions** have p95/p99 upper bounds **50/100 ms**, maximum **135.191 ms**. There are no pool-acquisition, Users/Auth, Worker database or closed-database failures; all **233 generic Match counts** equal domain conflicts. The probe has zero pool waiters, one blocked query and **22.863 ms** maximum transaction age, but **318 queued intents / 217 heartbeats**. Cleanup passes; release approval remains false.
- **Independent stress repeat:** unchanged `pnpm test:capacity:local --profile docs/testing/capacity-profile.mvp.json --stress-only` produces `ec308c09-afd3-456c-8323-34232396f79f.json`, exits one after **398.144 seconds**, and first fails a connected **match:catchup** acknowledgement in the 2x window. It admits **2,000 clients / 400 Matches**; warmup accepts **3,328/6,000** offers (**52.260 accepted commands/sec**), missing **2,672** busy offers. The window reaches the full population before turnover drops active Matches; it does not complete the stress/reconnect/recovery/soak gates. Total **26,047 offers / 12,161 accepted / 13,886 missed** (all busy), with **400 Matches / 11,896 receipts** reconciled before interruption. Room has **one normalized transaction conflict**, no Room INTERNAL_ERROR or HTTP 503; the first probe has **16,166 completed Room transactions**, p95/p99 upper bounds **50/100 ms**, maximum **89.581 ms**. There are no pool-acquisition, Users/Auth, Worker database or closed-database failures; all **173 final generic Match counts** match domain conflicts. At first failure **200 intents / 337 heartbeats** wait, with three pool waiters, no blocked query and **32.089 ms** maximum transaction age. Completed delivery service p95 upper bound **100 ms** and producer queue-age p95 upper bound **5,000 ms** do not isolate commit-to-all-recipient latency. Stress latency SLOs are not imposed. Cleanup passes; release approval remains false.
- **Completion / remaining gate:** the established Room SSI cycle is repaired and its targeted regressions pass; neither approved-profile repeat reproduces the exhausted Room admission error. Remaining catchup acknowledgement, gameplay backlog, normal turnover population/throughput and latency/reconnect failures belong to the existing **17CQ** investigation, which remains **Blocked at the mandatory resumption gate**, not Completed. **17CR is Completed locally; 17C remains Blocked; 18B3/19 stay Pending and staging fully deferred.** The exact next scope is to resume 17CQ from these artifacts and establish the remaining cause before any further repair or policy change. Full two-hour soak, service-failure recovery and production-like staging validation remain unproven. No dependency/lockfile, schema/index/grant, engine, public contract, retry count, timeout, quota, pool size, SLO or capacity target changes were made. No installation, fresh security scan, hosted CI, staged browser/Telegram smoke, publication, deployment or commit is claimed. Owned-resource audits leave only the pre-existing laptop resources; all prior dirty work is preserved.

### Phase 19 — Final Definition of Done and release-readiness review

- **Objective:** determine factually whether the HamBazi MVP is ready for public production release.
- **Scope:** architecture/game-module/MVP DoD audit, unresolved decisions/ADRs, complete validation evidence, security/capacity/operations results, residual risk and launch/hold recommendation.
- **Expected deliverables:** updated implementation plan, traceable readiness matrix, residual-risk register, and release decision input in the conversation.
- **Dependencies:** all prior phases; all launch-blocking open decisions, including `OD-001` through `OD-008` as applicable, require recorded owner outcomes.
- **Acceptance criteria:** every DoD item has evidence or is explicitly Blocked; no unapproved open decision is silently fixed; production launch is recommended only when all mandatory gates pass.
- **Required tests and validations:** full frozen-install/lint/typecheck/unit/integration/contract/build/E2E pipeline, security scans, replay checks, load/soak evidence, restore/rollback evidence, and final diff/document review.
- **Status:** Blocked (readiness assessment: hold; mandatory external staging/activation/owner evidence missing)

- **Authorized readiness assessment (2026-10-07):** evaluated the existing architecture/game-module/MVP DoD against current repository evidence after the 18B3 prerequisite assessment. Functional local Invite/Find → real Coup → Finish → Stats, deterministic engine/replay, atomic idempotency, authorization/private views and recovery pass the current 274-test/5-browser gate and 89/89-receipt smoke. Portable CI/image/deployment/rollback artifacts already exist. MVP 17C is accepted with exceptions, not a production performance certificate. Actual 7C secured operator controls/live composition, approved 18B3 staging/recovery, OD-005 public-beta policy and OD-008 rights remain absent. No production launch recommendation or blanket MVP launch-ready/completed-all-phases claim is made. Full fresh release scans/hosted enforcement/public HTTPS/mobile Telegram/provider restore/strict capacity soak are unperformed; dated earlier local evidence is retained. No unfinished phase is marked complete merely to advance the roadmap.

| Definition of Done area                         | Evidence / current readiness                                                                                              | Remaining release gate                                                                                        |
| ----------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Accounts, Invite/Find, Coup, chat, Finish/Stats | Completed local implementations; current built real-service browser paths pass                                            | Real Telegram/mobile/public HTTPS and approved live composition                                               |
| Pure game/versioning/replay/private views       | Existing complete module, retained engine versions and native replay/privacy/role checks pass                             | Exact active-pin/queue compatibility for a deployed release pair                                              |
| Durable writes/idempotency/recovery             | Current native concurrent/ambiguous-commit/rollback/lease/queue tests and fully reconciled smoke pass                     | Production-like faults, restore and workload validation                                                       |
| Security and operator maintenance               | Local transport/browser/scan policies implemented; player/service-shaped credentials cannot invoke absent admin mutations | 7C implementation plus approved MFA/RBAC/private operator access and audit policy                             |
| Capacity and SLO                                | 17C Completed for MVP with ADR-048 exceptions                                                                             | Deferred strict measurement/optimization/staging reconnect/fault/two-hour soak; no original SLO success claim |
| CI/packaging/migration/rollback                 | 18A/18B1/18B2 locally completed, earlier image/definition/replacement evidence retained                                   | Fresh exact-release scans, hosted protection/provenance and actual staging rollback                           |
| Backup, monitoring and privacy/rights           | Portable procedures, safe instrumentation and previous logical restore exist                                              | OD-002/005/006/remaining-007/008 and actual operating evidence                                                |

## Current gate

**Phase 17C is Completed for MVP with performance exceptions under the explicit
2026-10-07 owner decision and [ADR-048](adr/048-mvp-capacity-acceptance.md).**
The mandatory correctness, authorization, data-integrity, idempotency, recovery
and security implementation gates pass in the current tested checkpoint.
Current smoke reconciles 89/89 acknowledged receipts and three results/statistics;
native integration passes 274 tests and the built real-service browser passes five
cases. Large failed diagnostics and incomplete receipt coverage remain unchanged;
no production SLO, sustained peak, strict reconnect timing or two-hour soak claim
is made. Remaining 17CQ performance work and proposed 17CT measurement work are
explicitly deferred post-MVP. Numeric targets, budgets and runtime semantics stay
unchanged. No further local performance tuning is in progress.

The next authorized **18B3** is in bounded local/VPS preparation, with actual staging acceptance
**Blocked**. Temporary ParsPack Germany hosting and exact staging origins/Bot/DNS
are confirmed under [ADR-049](adr/049-temporary-parspack-staging-preparation.md).
Local read/write preparation is authorized. Sole ownership, disposable synthetic
personal data, no-backup/PITR/WAL-archive/offsite-storage exception and manual
monitoring are confirmed under ADR-050; recovery is excluded/unperformed, not passed.
ADR-051 authorizes bounded runner/network/private-file/TLS/image-transfer/observation
preparation. Networks/toolchain/private certificate identity and exact image transfer
are verified; actual role inputs, signal coverage, secured 7C and activation remain required.
The [operating worksheet](operations/staging-operating-decisions.md) records the
confirmed temporary inputs without inventing remaining policies. 18A, 18B1 and 18B2
remain Completed locally; parent 18B remains In Progress for actual staging.
Continuous phase authorization is retained; no generic approval is requested again.

The owner-authorized dependency remediation and local candidate validation are
complete at source `65ff180`; the dated 18B3 record above preserves exact inventory,
fresh scan and rehearsal evidence. Remaining concrete staging inputs and actual
deployment/rollback/manual-monitoring coverage are current blockers. Recovery is
excluded only for the disposable personal target under ADR-050, not passed. Freshness
must be checked again for any future release; successful local scans are not
protected hosted CI, registry provenance or staging acceptance.

The authorized **Phase 19 readiness assessment is Blocked / hold** for these
missing external gates, 7C secured operator implementation/activation policy,
OD-005 public-beta safety/privacy and OD-008 rights. Its evidence matrix above
separates the usable locally tested implementation from production launch
readiness. It does not falsely mark staging, 7C or Phase 19 complete. Original
OD-001 peaks and normal performance targets remain future optimization/staging
validation targets. Final operating decisions remain open.

The repository has implemented account/profile/catalog, Rooms/invites,
Matchmaking, pure Coup, atomic Match processing/outbox, reconnect/timeout/Bot
replacement, safe PlayerView UI, durable membership-protected chat and immutable
results/idempotent statistics. Both full product paths run through the declared
isolated release harness; the development preview is separately synthetic. The
public laptop composition still serves authenticated account/catalog capabilities
with production gameplay disabled pending 7C. It is not a publicly playable
release. Existing instructions and actual commands are in [release suites](testing/release-suites.md),
[Mini App shell](architecture/mini-app-shell.md) and [laptop operations](operations/laptop-server.md).

The earlier 17C acceptance/readiness review preserved its existing dirty work,
tracked `.aws` deletion, root environment and laptop durable volume, and added
documentation only. The current 18B3 follow-up preserves the resulting checkpoint
and adds the explicitly authorized dependency/preparation-metadata/documentation
remediation and local commits. Existing contracts, schema/grants, engines, approved targets and activation controls
remain unchanged. Owner-approved staging pools/network/grace are prepared separately
from unresolved role-profile proposals. No push, publication, application deployment,
public TLS, migration, real-data mutation or live Telegram action is performed.
Bounded remote runner/networks/private certificates/image imports are recorded in ADR-051.
